This is IMPLEMENTATION VALIDATION only — zero real graded recommendations exist yet in this environment, so EMPIRICAL VALIDATION (measured real-world performance improvement) remains explicitly pending — see Notes for full detail. **Pre-Phase-6 Operational Readiness Gate [Volume 2 v5.3] COMPLETE 2026-08-27** — Phase 5 Closeout Audit (verdict B) accepted, followed by a narrowly-scoped operational gate (explicitly NOT Milestone 5.6, NOT Phase 6): closed Master Refresh's total absence of an HTTP invocation path (new `POST /v1/internal/master-refresh/run`, sports-intel-layer) and built a finite Railway Cron Job dispatcher architecture (`apps/workers/app/cron_dispatch.py`, 4 DEV-only services — `cron-recommendation-worker`/`cron-postgame-grading`/`cron-adaptive-weighting`/`cron-master-refresh`) since Railway Cron Jobs cannot invoke an endpoint on an already-running server, so `worker-scheduled` stays always-on and unchanged. Closed the Recommendation Worker's pre-compute idempotency gap (`recommendations.cycle_completed_at`, skip-before-compute, DB state as sole authority) and extended Time Machine reconstruction to lifecycle/grade-history/weighting-evidence, prompt/model provenance surfacing, and delete-safety documentation (Volume 3 §5F). **Live DEV proof, honestly split:** Postgame Grading's full chain was genuinely fired out-of-schedule and completed for real (private-network call to `worker-scheduled`, `200 OK` confirmed on both sides, legitimate `no_candidates` zero-result); Recommendation Worker and Adaptive Weighting are config-verified identical in shape but were not independently live-fired this gate due to a disclosed Railway platform limitation (manual out-of-schedule cron triggering is unreliable — confirmed via Railway's own support agent, not a code defect); Master Refresh's endpoint is wired and tested but deliberately never fired against the live SportsDataIO provider, per Mac's explicit "wire but don't exercise" decision (the project's last reserved SportsDataIO Free Trial call, 11 of 12 already spent, stays unspent). A real empirical finding was fixed along the way: Railway injects `PORT=8080` at runtime on every service, silently overriding each Dockerfile's own `${PORT:-8000}` fallback — discovered via a live connection failure, confirmed via Railway's own deploy logs, corrected project-wide for the new internal URLs. A separate, real, pre-existing infrastructure risk was found and disclosed (not fixed, out of this gate's scope): four already-running services (`worker-scheduled`/`ai-orchestrator`/`sports-intel-layer`/`api-gateway`) have no explicit source branch pinned for dev, defaulting to the repo's stale `main` branch — echoes a real 2026-08-13 incident, flagged for a dedicated future fix. ## Notes

- Full blueprint document set is complete: all five volumes, `v2.0-amendments-architecture-review.md`, `engineering-roadmap-build-order.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` are in place and internally consistent as of CHANGELOG v2.0.2.
- **Railway is connected and provisioned.** Project `theplaybook` (workspace `theplaybook-sys's Projects`) has dev/staging/production environments and all 6 services, each with `rootDirectory` correctly scoped to its `apps/<service>` subdirectory.
- **2026-08-06 — Deploy-blocking bugs found and fixed (all 6 services, dev + staging):**
  1. **Builder misconfiguration.** All deployments failed with "Detected Python / Using pip / No start command detected." Root cause: Railway's `dockerfilePath` setting alone is not sufficient — the separate `build.builder` field must be explicitly set to `DOCKERFILE`, or Railway silently defaults to Railpack auto-detection regardless of `dockerfilePath`. Fixed directly on all 6 Railway services in both environments (Railway-side config, not a repo change).
  2. **Frontend CVE block.** `next@14.2.15` carried two HIGH-severity CVEs (CVE-2025-55184, CVE-2025-67779); Railway's dependency scanner refused to build until patched. Fixed in PR #1: bumped to `next@14.2.35`.
  3. **Healthcheck failures post-build.** Once the builder fix took effect, all 4 Python services' Dockerfiles still failed their `/health` checks — they hardcoded `--port 8000` instead of reading Railway's injected `$PORT`, so Railway's healthcheck (which always probes `$PORT`) timed out. Fixed in PR #1: `CMD ["sh","-c","uvicorn app.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port ${PORT:-8000}"]` in all 4 Python Dockerfiles.
  - PR #1 (`claude/playbook-phase0-railway-h5ohzr` → `dev`) merged 2026-08-06; CI green (5/5 test jobs); merge auto-triggered fresh deploys. All 12 dev+staging deployments confirmed `SUCCESS` via both the Railway API and the dashboard.
- **Supabase connector not yet installed.** Not required for Phase 0, but will block Phase 1 (Database Foundation) at that phase's start.
- **2026-08-06 — Sentry wired for all 4 Python services.** Mac provided a Sentry DSN (FastAPI project). Set as `SENTRY_DSN` on `api-gateway`, `ai-orchestrator`, `sports-intel-layer`, `worker-scheduled`, and `worker-market-monitor` (the two Railway services built from `apps/workers`) across dev/staging/production — same pattern as other API keys, Railway env var, never committed. Each service's `app/main.py` calls `sentry_sdk.init(dsn=os.environ.get("SENTRY_DSN"), send_default_pii=False)` before app construction, with `sentry-sdk[fastapi]` added to each `requirements.txt`.
  - `send_default_pii` is `False`, not `True` as in the initial pass — no privacy policy is live yet to disclose PII collection per Volume 1 §10; revisit once one exists.
  - `/sentry-debug` (the division-by-zero verification route from Sentry's own setup docs) is gated to only register when `RAILWAY_ENVIRONMENT_NAME` is `dev` (or unset, for local runs) — it does not exist on staging or production builds.
  - **Frontend Sentry deferred to Phase 6, confirmed by Mac 2026-08-06 — not skipped.** `apps/frontend` currently only has the Phase 0 CI/CD skeleton (`app/api/health/route.ts`, a bare `layout.tsx`/`page.tsx`) with no real product UI yet; the actual frontend codebase (dashboards, chat interface) isn't built until Phase 6 (`docs/blueprint/engineering-roadmap-build-order.md`). There's nothing meaningful for the Sentry JS SDK to attach to right now. The roadmap's Phase 0 acceptance criterion literally reads "captures and reports a deliberately-triggered test error in **each service**" — Mac's explicit call is that this is satisfied by the four backend services that exist today, and frontend Sentry gets wired as part of Phase 6's build-out, not backfilled onto the empty skeleton now. Flagging this explicitly per CLAUDE.md's blueprint-vs-reality process so it's a decision on record, not a silently dropped criterion: **action item for Phase 6 — wire Sentry JS SDK into the real frontend app when it's built.**
- **2026-08-07 — GitHub Actions runner-queue issue resolved.** Earlier in the session, workflow runs were stuck at `queued` indefinitely ("All GitHub-hosted runners with label [ubuntu-latest] are busy"), confirmed infrastructure-wide via a minimal diagnostic workflow. As of this entry, fresh runs start and complete normally (test jobs finish in ~10-20s each, no queueing). `www.githubstatus.com` could not be checked directly — this environment's outbound proxy denies that host by policy (403 on CONNECT) — so resolution is confirmed empirically via multiple real runs completing cleanly, not via GitHub's own status page.
- **2026-08-07 — `RAILWAY_TOKEN` is set and deploy jobs now actually run — and they're broken.** `RAILWAY_TOKEN` was set as a GitHub Actions environment secret earlier in the session (superseding the "still not set" note below, kept for history). This was the first time `ci-cd.yml`'s deploy jobs ran for real rather than being skipped, and re-running the earlier stuck dev-branch push (commit `5cc05a4`) surfaced two independent, previously-invisible defects — masked until now because dev/staging deploys had been happening via Railway's own GitHub auto-deploy, not the Actions pipeline:
  1. **`deploy-dev (workers)` fails outright: `Service not found`.** The matrix uses `workers` as the Railway `--service` name, but no Railway service is named `workers` — the codebase at `apps/workers` deploys as two separate Railway services, `worker-scheduled` and `worker-market-monitor`. Neither name matches the matrix entry.
  2. **The other 4 deploy jobs (`api-gateway`, `ai-orchestrator`, `sports-intel-layer`, `frontend`) report `success` in GitHub Actions but the Railway deployment they trigger actually fails.** `railway up --detach` only confirms the deploy was *triggered*, not that it *succeeded* — the Actions job exits 0 immediately without waiting for the build. Checked directly against the Railway API: all 4 resulting deployments show `status: FAILED`. Root cause (from `api-gateway`'s build log): `railway up` runs with `working-directory: apps/<service>`, uploading only that subfolder as the build snapshot — but each Railway service's `rootDirectory` is configured as `apps/<service>` (correct for git-push-triggered deploys, which upload the whole repo), so the builder then tries to resolve `apps/<service>` *inside* a snapshot that's already scoped to just that folder, and fails: `lstat .../apps: no such file or directory`.
  - **Net effect:** the CI-gates-deploy path had never actually worked end-to-end for any service via GitHub Actions — Phase 0's "trivial change pushed to `dev` deploys automatically" acceptance criterion had only ever been satisfied by Railway's native autodeploy, not by the Actions pipeline that's supposed to gate it on tests passing. Logged per CLAUDE.md's blueprint-vs-reality process rather than silently patched.
  - **Verified dev's live traffic was unaffected during the broken window — confirmed fact, not assumption.** Pulled `deploy` logs directly from each service's last-`SUCCESS` deployment prior to the broken run: `api-gateway`, `ai-orchestrator`, and `sports-intel-layer` each show `Starting Container` followed by a self-issued `GET /health HTTP/1.1 200 OK`; `frontend` shows a clean Next.js `Ready in 180ms` startup. All 4 failed deploy attempts died during the build/snapshot-unpack stage (`no such file or directory`) — before Docker ever built an image or Railway attempted to start a container — so Railway had nothing to swap traffic to and kept serving the last healthy container throughout. `worker-scheduled`/`worker-market-monitor` weren't touched at all by the broken run (it failed before even reaching `railway up`, since the service name didn't resolve), so they were never at risk.
  - **Fix approved and applied by Mac 2026-08-07.** `.github/workflows/ci-cd.yml`: dropped `working-directory: apps/${{ matrix.service }}` from all three deploy jobs (`deploy-dev`, `deploy-staging`, `deploy-production` — the identical bug was present in all three, not just `deploy-dev`; caught and fixed in this same pass before it could surface separately at a staging/production promotion) so `railway up` now uploads the full repo from checkout root and lets each service's configured `rootDirectory` do the scoping, matching how autodeploy already works. Split the `workers` matrix entry into `worker-scheduled` and `worker-market-monitor` in all three jobs.
  - **Verified against the Railway API directly, not the Actions checkmark** (same check that caught the original bug): pushed the fix straight to `dev` to exercise `deploy-dev` for real. All 6 resulting deployments — `api-gateway`, `ai-orchestrator`, `sports-intel-layer`, `worker-scheduled`, `worker-market-monitor`, `frontend` — confirmed `status: SUCCESS` via `mcp__Railway__get-status` and each service's own deploy logs (container started, healthcheck passing) after the builds finished. `deploy-staging`/`deploy-production` remain untriggered (by design — staging needs a `main` push, production needs a `v*` tag) but now carry the same fix and will be exercised for real the next time either path fires.
- "Wait for CI" / "disable autodeploy" toggles are dashboard-only settings, not exposed via the Railway MCP tools available in this session — needed so a failing test actually blocks a dev/staging deploy per Phase 0's testing requirements. Not yet configured; will need Mac to set these in the Railway dashboard, or a fallback approach flagged if that turns out to be blocking.
- Logged per CLAUDE.md's blueprint-vs-reality process: the builder-field and `$PORT` gotchas above are infra/build-tool behavior, not a blueprint deviation, so no CHANGELOG entry — Volume 2 §5/§9's Dockerfile-per-service approach is unchanged, this was purely a Railway configuration correction.
- **2026-08-07 — Production outage: 5/6 services down, found during the Phase 0 closure audit, fixed same-day.**
  - **Discovery:** the closure audit's independent verification (checking the Railway API directly rather than trusting PROGRESS.md's own prior entries) found `api-gateway`, `ai-orchestrator`, `sports-intel-layer`, `worker-scheduled`, and `worker-market-monitor` all at `status: FAILED` in production, each stuck on deployment `753d09f4` — a stale pre-Phase-0-fixes snapshot whose container was still running the old hardcoded `--port 8000` and failing healthchecks for 5 minutes before Railway gave up and stopped it. `frontend` was unaffected (last real deploy 2026-08-06, untouched by this).
  - **Root cause:** an earlier `mcp__Railway__set-variables` call this session (setting `SENTRY_DSN` on production services) triggered a default redeploy. Production has autodeploy intentionally disabled (no `source.branch` configured, correct per Volume 2 §9 — production should only deploy via tagged releases), so that redeploy had no live git source to build from and fell back to replaying the last cached build snapshot — the broken pre-fix one.
  - **Fix, approved and applied by Mac 2026-08-07:** used `railway-agent`'s `deployServiceTool` to trigger a one-off deployment for each of the 5 broken services, in production, built from GitHub commit `51b68d5584f228c3bfe19b977a418586855a4771` (current `dev` tip — has the builder/$PORT/CI-deploy fixes; deliberately *not* PR #3's still-unmerged content, since production shouldn't receive unreviewed changes as a side effect of an outage fix). Confirmed each service's persisted `source` config still has no `branch` key afterward — autodeploy remains disabled on production exactly as before, this was a one-time deploy-from-commit, not a standing config change.
  - **Verified against the Railway API directly, not the dashboard or a checkmark:** all 6 production services now show `status: SUCCESS` via `mcp__Railway__get-status`, and each of the 5 fixed services' own `deploy` logs confirm a started container bound to Railway's assigned port (8080, not the old hardcoded 8000) with `/health` returning `200 OK` — and for `api-gateway` specifically, the `build` log was also checked directly (not just `get-service-config`, which misreported `builder: RAILPACK` for this one service — the same config-API staleness noted elsewhere in this file) and confirms the deploy genuinely used the Dockerfile.
  - **Standing fix, not just a one-time patch:** added a new section to `CLAUDE.md` — "Railway Config Mutations: Default to `skipDeploys: true`" — requiring every Railway config/variable mutation call to pass `skipDeploys: true` unless a deploy is the explicit point of that call, so a routine variable-set can't silently redeploy an autodeploy-disabled environment from a stale snapshot again. Applied immediately as a self-check: locked `api-gateway` production's `builder` field back to `DOCKERFILE` explicitly (correcting the stale-read config) using `skipDeploys: true`, confirmed via `get-status` that this did not trigger a new deployment on top of the already-healthy one.
- **2026-08-07 — Dual-trigger race confirmed real, all three environments hardened to a single deploy path.** Investigated on Mac's explicit request rather than assumed safe: `dev` had both Railway's native git-autodeploy and the Actions `deploy-dev` job firing on every push. Confirmed via the Railway API this was a real, observed defect, not theoretical — two deployments for the same commit were live simultaneously for ~38 seconds. Fixed by disabling Railway's native git-autodeploy on all three environments (`dev`, `staging`, `production`); only the Actions-gated `railway up` step deploys anywhere now, matching production's pre-existing pattern. Staging's `source.branch` also corrected to track `main` (was drifting from `dev`) as part of the same pass. Verified against the Railway API directly post-fix: all three environments' `source` config carries no `branch` key (disabled state), and only one deployment record is created per push. Logged in `CHANGELOG.md` as v4.1 (MINOR, Volume 2 only) per CLAUDE.md's blueprint-vs-reality process — Volume 2 §9's body updated in the same pass, not header-only.
- **2026-08-07 — Sentry environment-tagging defect found and fixed.** Real end-to-end verification (triggering `/sentry-debug` against a live `dev` instance and checking the actual Sentry dashboard, not just confirming the code path exists) found every event tagged `environment: production` regardless of which Railway environment actually generated it — `sentry_sdk.init()` never set `environment=`, so the SDK silently defaulted to `"production"` for all four backend services on every environment. Fixed by adding `environment=os.environ.get("RAILWAY_ENVIRONMENT_NAME", "dev")` to all four `sentry_sdk.init()` calls (commit `94feda8`); re-verified via a fresh `/sentry-debug` trigger on `api-gateway` showing `environment: dev` in the Sentry Tags panel. Also logged in the same `CHANGELOG.md` v4.1 entry.
- **2026-08-07 — TR1 and TR2 (Phase 0 testing requirements) executed and independently verified.**
  - **TR1 (failing test blocks deploy):** a deliberately-failing test was committed and pushed to `dev` (commit `2979ef5`); confirmed via the Actions job graph that `deploy-dev` jobs were never even created (the `needs:` dependency on the failed test job blocked job-graph evaluation entirely) and via the Railway API that no new deployment record was created for any of the 6 dev services. Reverted (commit `bf9fef9`); confirmed 5/5 tests passed and all 6 `deploy-dev` jobs succeeded, all 6 services `SUCCESS` via the Railway API.
  - **TR2 (manual rollback works):** a manual dashboard rollback was performed on `api-gateway` (dev), since neither the direct Railway MCP tools nor `railway-agent` expose the underlying `deploymentRollback` GraphQL mutation for this session to call directly. Verified against the Railway API afterward: the resulting deployment's `meta.reason` was `"rollback"` (confirming the real rollback mutation was used, not a rebuild) and its `snapshotId` exactly matched the target deployment's snapshot (confirming a byte-identical image restore). `api-gateway` was then forward-restored back to `dev` HEAD (`bf9fef9`); confirmed `SUCCESS` via the Railway API, with the build log inspected directly (not the `get-service-config` `builder` field, which is known-stale and misreports `RAILPACK`) to confirm a genuine Dockerfile build, and a passing healthcheck.
- **2026-08-07 — AC2 (network isolation) accepted on structural grounds, not an active test.** Verified: all three Railway environments exist with all 6 services each, all currently `SUCCESS` (dev, staging, production independently confirmed via the Railway API same day). Isolation itself — that nothing in one environment can reach another — was accepted because nothing in the repo or Railway config creates cross-environment networking, not because an active probe (e.g., attempting a cross-environment connection and confirming it's refused) was ever run. This is an accepted assumption, not a tested guarantee. Tracked as an Immediate item in the Engineering Roadmap's Technical Debt & Feature Backlog (`engineering-roadmap-build-order.md`) — "Active cross-environment isolation test."
- **2026-08-07 — AC3 (error tracking, each service) fully verified.** `/sentry-debug` was hit against a live `dev` instance for all four backend services and confirmed landing in the Sentry dashboard, each tagged `environment: dev`: `api-gateway` (first, caught the environment-mislabeling defect), then `ai-orchestrator`, `sports-intel-layer`, and `worker-market-monitor` (public dev domains generated for these three specifically to make this check possible — none previously had one). Mac independently confirmed all three remaining events' Tags panels. AC3 is now satisfied for every backend service, not just one.
- **2026-08-07 — PR #4 merged into `main` (real merge commit `02695f5`, not fast-forward); `deploy-staging` fired for real for the first time and verified.** `main` had been sitting at the original Phase 0 scaffold commit since before any Phase 0 fixes existed; this merge is the first time it advanced. The push to `main` triggered `deploy-staging` for real for the first time ever (previously only exercised on `dev`) — all 6 `deploy-staging` jobs in Actions run `31207884080` completed `success` against head_sha `02695f5`, with `deploy-dev`/`deploy-production` correctly `skipped` (this was a push to `main`, not `dev`, and not a tag). Verified against the Railway API directly, not the Actions checkmark: all 6 staging services confirmed `SUCCESS`, with deployment creation timestamps (18:39:12–18:39:26) matching the Actions job execution window precisely. (Railway's CLI-triggered `railway up` deployments don't carry a `commitHash` in their metadata the way git-webhook deploys do — expected now that native autodeploy is disabled everywhere — so the job-timestamp correlation is the verification method here, not a `meta.commitHash` field.)
- **2026-08-07 — `deploy-production` (the tag-gated path) has never fired for real.** Production's current healthy state (all 6 services `SUCCESS`) came from the manual outage-response deploy on 2026-08-07 (`deployServiceTool`, one-off, from a specific commit) — not from the intended `deploy-production` Actions job, which only triggers on a `v*` tag push and has never actually run. The `working-directory`/service-name fixes applied to `deploy-production` are identical to the ones verified working in `deploy-dev` and (as of this entry) `deploy-staging`, so there's good reason to expect it works, but "good reason to expect" is not the same standard used everywhere else in this file. **Action item: the first time a real production-bound tag is pushed, verify `deploy-production` against the Railway API directly, the same way `deploy-dev` and `deploy-staging` were verified here — don't assume it works because the other two paths did.**
- **2026-08-07 — `v0.0.1-phase0` tag push blocked by a GitHub-side ref protection; investigated, not bypassed, handed to Mac.** Pushing the annotated tag (`git push origin v0.0.1-phase0`) failed with `HTTP 403` / `send-pack: unexpected disconnect` — a clean per-ref rejection, not a connection or auth failure. Investigated before concluding anything: the agent proxy's `recentRelayFailures` log was empty (request reached GitHub, wasn't blocked by session egress policy), and the same push identity (`ThePlaybook-sys`, the repo owner) had successfully pushed two branches moments earlier via the same mechanism — ruling out a general credential or collaborator-permission problem. This narrows it to a GitHub-side tag protection rule or repository ruleset targeting `v*`/`refs/tags/**` (the repo has zero existing tags, consistent with either, and this session's tooling has no working credential to query `tags/protection` or `rulesets` directly to distinguish which). **This is an intentional protection, not a bug — not bypassed or worked around.** Per Mac's decision: he will create and push `v0.0.1-phase0` himself, from an account/context with the necessary bypass permission. The local unpushed tag object created during the investigation was deleted (`git tag -d`) to avoid any confusion with whatever Mac creates. **Once Mac's tag lands, verify `deploy-production` against the Railway API directly (status, deploy logs, healthchecks — same standard as dev/staging), then update this file's `deploy-production` and tag-status notes from "not yet done" to "verified," with evidence.**
- **2026-08-07 — Phase 0 audit approved by Mac.** Explicit sign-off given on the second re-run of the final Phase 0 validation (all AC1–AC3, TR1–TR2 independently verified; AC2 isolation accepted as a logged assumption; CHANGELOG v4.1 written). Mac's explicit instruction: prepare the repository for closure, but **do not begin Phase 1** and **wait for the production release tag** — he is creating `v0.0.1-phase0` manually himself. Phase 1 has NOT been started as of this entry.
- **2026-08-07 — `v0.0.1-phase0` release verified end-to-end, final independent audit.** Mac pushed the tag himself (past the ref protection documented above) pointing at `main`'s tip, commit `cc66f8d2d8969e5db7a90d9198299e0d7267c5f9`. Verified independently, Railway API first, GitHub second, not the checkmark alone:
  1. **Workflow completed successfully:** Actions run `31211866111` (event `push`, `head_branch: v0.0.1-phase0`, `head_sha: cc66f8d2`) — all 5 test jobs and all 6 `deploy-production (<service>)` jobs `conclusion: success`; `deploy-dev`/`deploy-staging` correctly `skipped` (this was a tag push, not a branch push).
  2. **All 6 production services `SUCCESS`** via `mcp__Railway__get-status` on the production environment directly: `api-gateway`, `ai-orchestrator`, `sports-intel-layer`, `worker-scheduled`, `worker-market-monitor`, `frontend` — deployment timestamps (19:32:26–19:32:32) match the Actions job execution window (19:32:20–19:32:34) precisely.
  3. **Commit correlation confirmed:** the `deploy-production` jobs' own `head_sha` is `cc66f8d2`, identical to the tag's commit. Railway's CLI-triggered deployments don't carry a `commitHash` in their metadata (expected, same as dev/staging all session, since native autodeploy is disabled everywhere) — verified instead via `api-gateway`'s build log directly: genuine Dockerfile build (`[1/5] FROM python:3.11-slim` through `[5/5] COPY app ./app`, not Nixpacks/Railpack) that started seconds after the job's checkout of that exact SHA, plus a passing healthcheck (`Healthcheck succeeded!`, `/health` → `200 OK`).
  4. **No failed deployment remains as latest:** `get-status`'s `latestDeployment` is `SUCCESS` for all 6 services; cross-checked via `list-deployments` on `api-gateway` — the prior `FAILED` deployment (from the original outage) and the prior `SUCCESS` (from the manual outage-response fix) are both `REMOVED`, properly superseded by the new one.
  - **Phase 0 is now officially CLOSED AND RELEASED.** All acceptance criteria and testing requirements independently verified with real evidence, not assumptions or checkmarks, across dev, staging, and now production. Remaining non-blocking informational items: AC2 isolation remains an accepted structural assumption (tracked in the Technical Debt Backlog, Immediate); frontend Sentry remains deferred to Phase 6 per Mac's 2026-08-06 decision.
- **2026-08-07 — Phase 1 started; Supabase three-project environment architecture provisioned.** Mac authorized the Supabase connector, then approved the intended architecture explicitly (Volume 3 §12 requires `dev`/`staging`/`production` as fully separate Supabase projects, not schemas within one — same principle as Railway's environment split). Findings and actions:
  - Org (`ThePlaybook-sys's Org`) confirmed already on the **Pro plan** — no upgrade needed, despite an earlier `get_cost` check (before this authorization) showing $0/month, which turned out to reflect a different quote than the $10/month/project Pro-tier cost actually charged at creation time. Cost was explicitly stated to Mac ($20/month for the two new projects) before creating anything, consistent with the tool's own required confirmation step.
  - **`staging`** (`theplaybook-staging`, ref `jhpjdjtvzzmhxvprsfaq`, `db.jhpjdjtvzzmhxvprsfaq.supabase.co`, us-east-1) and **`production`** (`theplaybook-production`, ref `dronhltumzkngwwktesf`, `db.dronhltumzkngwwktesf.supabase.co`, us-east-1) created; the pre-existing project (`nhwjtsdebgiwskshzqiq`, `db.nhwjtsdebgiwskshzqiq.supabase.co`) mapped to **`dev`**. All three independently confirmed `ACTIVE_HEALTHY` via `mcp__Supabase__get_project`, with three distinct project IDs and three distinct database hosts — full separation verified, not assumed.
  - `SUPABASE_URL` set on all 6 services in each of the 3 Railway environments (18 calls total), and `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` set on `frontend` in each environment — all with `skipDeploys: true`. Independently verified via `mcp__Railway__list-variables` (spot-checked `api-gateway`/dev, `frontend`/staging, `sports-intel-layer`/production) and confirmed no environment's deployment timestamps changed (`skipDeploys` held, no accidental redeploy — the same failure mode as the original production outage).
  - **`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` deliberately not set yet** — Supabase's MCP tooling exposes only publishable/anon keys, not the service role key, same principle as any other high-sensitivity credential in this project (CLAUDE.md's Credentials & Connections section). Mac needs to retrieve each project's service role key from its own dashboard and provide it once per environment; documented as an open item in `docs/ops/secrets-management.md`.
  - **Correction found and fixed in `docs/ops/secrets-management.md`:** the existing secret inventory table listed the service role key as needed by "API Gateway, Orchestrator, Workers" only, omitting `sports-intel-layer` — but Volume 2 §4.3 has it normalizing and writing provider data into the internal data model, so it needs DB write access too. Fixed in the same pass; this is an ops-doc completeness fix, not a blueprint deviation, so no CHANGELOG entry.
  - Full project-to-environment mapping table now lives in `docs/ops/secrets-management.md`.
  - **Schema migrations have not started** — holding per Mac's explicit instruction to report the final configuration first.
- **2026-08-07 — SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY set on all backend services, all three environments.** Mac confirmed the reported configuration and provided all three service role keys directly. Each key's JWT payload was decoded and checked before use — `role: service_role` and `ref` matching the intended project (production key → `dronhltumzkngwwktesf`, staging → `jhpjdjtvzzmhxvprsfaq`, dev → `nhwjtsdebgiwskshzqiq`) — confirming no keys were mismatched or swapped between environments. Set on `api-gateway`, `ai-orchestrator`, `sports-intel-layer`, `worker-scheduled`, `worker-market-monitor` in each environment (15 calls, `skipDeploys: true`). Independently verified via `list-variables` (spot-checked dev/api-gateway, staging/sports-intel-layer, production/worker-scheduled, production/worker-market-monitor) and via `get-status` on production confirming deployment timestamps unchanged (no accidental redeploy). Supabase environment configuration is now fully complete — proceeding to Milestone 1 (core user/account tables).
- **2026-08-07 — Milestone 1 (core user/account tables) written, applied to `dev`, and verified against all five agreed acceptance criteria.** Migration `supabase/migrations/20260807205946_core_user_account_tables.sql` (Supabase CLI convention per Volume 3 §12 — one SQL file, sequentially numbered, committed to the repo) creates `user_profiles`, `subscriptions`, `betting_dna`, applied via `mcp__Supabase__apply_migration` to the dev project (`nhwjtsdebgiwskshzqiq`), which was empty beforehand — the required replay-test precondition.
  1. **Schema correctness:** verified via `mcp__Supabase__list_tables` (not just the migration tool's own success response) — all columns, types, check constraints, defaults, and FKs to `auth.users` match Volume 3 §3 exactly, including the v2.0 `referral_code`/`deleted_at` additions on `user_profiles`.
  2. **RLS — actual cross-user read test, not just policy existence:** two real test users were inserted into `auth.users` inside a transaction, with one profile/subscription/betting_dna row each. Session context was switched to `role authenticated` with `request.jwt.claims` set to simulate User A, then User A queried User B's rows directly. Result: **0 rows** returned for all three tables (`user_profiles`, `subscriptions`, `betting_dna`), while User A's own rows returned **1 row** each in the same test — confirming RLS is actively filtering, not failing open or simply erroring. The whole test ran inside `begin`/`rollback`; a follow-up `execute_sql` call independently confirmed all three tables and the two test `auth.users` rows are back to 0 — dev is unpolluted.
  3. **Soft-delete filtering:** `own_profile_select` on `user_profiles` includes `and deleted_at is null`; confirmed `subscriptions`/`betting_dna` have no `deleted_at` column and no filter was added there, per scope.
  4. **`set_updated_at()` trigger:** applied to `user_profiles` and `subscriptions` only (both have `updated_at`); `betting_dna`'s `last_recalculated_at` is a different column and was correctly left out of scope.
  5. **Migration replay:** applying to `dev` from its confirmed-empty state (checked via `list_tables` before running) **is** the replay test for this first migration, per the agreed acceptance criteria — satisfied.
  - **Flag carried over from the prior session and resolved in the migration itself, not silently assumed:** `subscriptions` is not in Volume 3 §10's explicit named list of tables using the `auth.uid() = user_id` RLS pattern. Applied anyway per §10's opening line ("RLS is enabled on every table containing user data") since `subscriptions` holds user-specific billing data — documented as an inline comment in the migration file itself (not just here) so the reasoning travels with the schema, per CLAUDE.md's blueprint-vs-reality process. Not a CHANGELOG-worthy deviation (no blueprint text was contradicted, just a gap in its enumerated list) — captured as an implementation note.
  - Neither `subscriptions` nor `betting_dna` got a user-facing `insert`/`update`/`delete` policy: both are written exclusively by system processes (billing integration, background worker per Volume 3 §3) via the service-role key, which bypasses RLS by design (§10) — no policy needed for access the app doesn't grant to end users.
  - `staging`/`production` have **not** been touched — Volume 3 §12's dev → staging → production migration path only promotes after dev is proven, which this entry establishes.
- **2026-08-07 — Milestone 2 (sports data tables, Volume 3 §4) scoped, decided, applied to `dev`, and verified.** Given the size of this milestone (~25 tables spanning §4.0's normalized multi-sport core, `games`, 5 append-only snapshot tables, `daily_game_intelligence`, and the 13 §4.2 derived score tables), two real design gaps not settled by blueprint text were flagged to Mac before writing any SQL, per CLAUDE.md's blueprint-vs-reality process, and decided:
  1. **RLS scope for non-user tables.** §10 literally scopes RLS to "every table containing user data," but none of §4's tables hold user data, and Volume 2 confirms the frontend reads some of it directly via Supabase Realtime rather than exclusively through the API Gateway — meaning RLS is the only enforcement layer in front of those reads. **Decision (Mac, 2026-08-07): enable RLS on every §4 table with a permissive public-read policy** (`for select using (true)`), writes reserved for the service role only. A clarifying note was added to Volume 3 §10 itself (not just here) — this section's "every table containing user data" should be read as "every table requiring access control"; §4's tables need RLS for a different reason (Realtime exposure) than user-data protection. Documentation clarification only, no CHANGELOG entry (nothing in the blueprint was contradicted, just a gap in its stated scope filled in) — same precedent as the `sports-intel-layer` ops-doc fix.
  2. **Append-only enforcement scope.** §4 describes `odds_snapshots`, `injury_reports`, `weather_snapshots`, `depth_chart_snapshots`, and `referee_assignments` as "append-only by design," but §11 only specifies a DB-level trigger for `recommendation_agent_outputs` (a §5/Milestone-3 table). **Decision (Mac, 2026-08-07): extend the same DB-level enforcement to all five §4 snapshot tables now**, for the same reasoning that motivated the original trigger. Implemented as one shared `block_snapshot_updates()` function (parameterized on `TG_TABLE_NAME`) rather than five near-identical functions, with a `before update` trigger on each of the five tables — matches the original trigger's scope exactly (blocks `UPDATE` only, not `DELETE`, no broader scope invented).
  - Migration: `supabase/migrations/20260807211306_sports_data_tables.sql`, applied via `mcp__Supabase__apply_migration` on top of Milestone 1's already-applied schema.
  - **Schema correctness:** verified via `mcp__Supabase__list_tables` — all 28 new tables present (9 normalized-core + `games`, 5 snapshot tables, `daily_game_intelligence`, 13 derived score tables), `rls_enabled: true` on every one, alongside the 3 Milestone 1 tables (31 total).
  - **Column-shape note, flagged rather than silently invented:** §4 doesn't name exact payload columns for `injury_reports`/`weather_snapshots`/`depth_chart_snapshots`/`referee_assignments` beyond "mirrors `odds_snapshots`' shape... keyed to `game_id`, with `captured_at`." Extrapolated a single normalized jsonb payload column per table (`report_data`, `weather_data`, `depth_chart_data`, `assignment_data`), following `odds_snapshots`' own adapter-normalized-jsonb pattern rather than inventing sport-specific columns — documented inline in the migration file itself.
  - **`games` dual-write proof (explicit Phase 1 AC):** seeded a test `sports`/`leagues`/`seasons`/`games` chain inside a transaction — confirmed the resulting `games` row has `sport = 'nfl'` (legacy field) and `sport_id` correctly referencing the seeded `sports.id` simultaneously, proving the backward-compatible dual-write actually works, not just that both columns exist.
  - **Public-read RLS actually verified, not just policy existence:** within the same test transaction, switched to `role anon` and confirmed real reads succeed — `games`, `sports`, and `odds_snapshots` each returned their expected row count under the `anon` role, proving the public-read policies actually grant access rather than merely existing unverified.
  - **Append-only trigger verified by attempting real `UPDATE`s, not just checking the trigger exists:** representative tests against `odds_snapshots` and `injury_reports` (two of the five, exercising the shared trigger function against two different `TG_TABLE_NAME` values) — each `UPDATE` attempt was wrapped in a `DO` block that re-raises unless the caught error matches the append-only exception text; both passed silently, meaning both updates were correctly blocked. Had either update succeeded or failed for an unrelated reason, the block would have surfaced a hard error instead.
  - **Dev confirmed unpolluted:** all test data was inserted inside `begin`/`rollback`; a follow-up `execute_sql` call independently confirmed `sports`, `leagues`, `seasons`, `games`, `odds_snapshots`, and `injury_reports` are all back to 0 rows.
  - **Not done in this milestone, by design:** the full Phase-1-level testing requirements (automated RLS test suite, migration reversibility check, seed script for Phase 3–5 development) are Phase 1-level requirements per the roadmap, not per-milestone — same treatment as Milestone 1. `staging`/`production` untouched.
- **2026-08-07 — Self-caught bug: Milestone 2's `odds_snapshots` was built on UUIDv4, not the required UUIDv7 — found and fixed before Milestone 3 began.** While researching UUID generation ahead of building `recommendation_agent_outputs` (which the v2.0 amendment also requires on UUIDv7), found that Milestone 2's `odds_snapshots` had been built with plain `gen_random_uuid()` instead. Confirmed dev runs PostgreSQL 17.6, which has no native `uuidv7()` (lands in PG18) and no extension provides one either. Fixed via `supabase/migrations/20260807213221_uuidv7_function_and_odds_snapshots_fix.sql`: a custom `uuid_generate_v7()` PL/pgSQL function, then `alter table odds_snapshots alter column id set default uuid_generate_v7()`. Verified with a real insert (rolled back after): the generated UUID (`019fde24-813b-7a98-af00-c52aeeed0c0d`) carries the correct version nibble (`7`), a valid RFC 4122 variant (`a`), and an embedded timestamp within 17ms of wall-clock time. No backfill needed — `odds_snapshots` had zero real rows. Logged as **CHANGELOG v4.1.1, PATCH, Volume 3 only** (per Mac's explicit instruction — this closes a gap in the already-approved v2.0 UUIDv7 decision, not a new architectural decision); Volume 3's header bumped from v4.0 to v4.1.1 to match. The same function is reused for `recommendation_agent_outputs` below, and will be reused again for `market_monitoring_events` in Milestone 4.
- **2026-08-07 — Milestone 3 (AI intelligence tables, Volume 3 §5 only) scoped, applied to `dev`, and verified.** Scope confirmed explicitly before writing code: `feature_flags`, `prompt_registry`, `model_registry`, `recommendation_costs`, and the expanded `audit_log` are labeled a "v2.0 addition" in the roadmap's Phase 1 blurb but actually live in Volume 3 §8 (AI Orchestration Config) — they belong to **Milestone 4** ("§6–§8"), not this one, and were deliberately left out.
  - Migration: `supabase/migrations/20260807213325_ai_intelligence_tables.sql` — `agents`, `agent_performance_scores`, `recommendations`, `recommendation_agent_outputs` (on `uuid_generate_v7()`), `consensus_snapshots`, `explainability_payloads`, `recommendation_snapshots`. Applied cleanly on top of the UUIDv7 fix migration.
  - **RLS design decision, flagged rather than silently chosen:** `recommendations` gets the exact tier-gated select policy §10 already specifies verbatim, combined with the soft-delete filter (§10 explicitly names `recommendations` in its soft-delete-filtering list). The other six tables (`agents`, `agent_performance_scores`, `recommendation_agent_outputs`, `consensus_snapshots`, `explainability_payloads`, `recommendation_snapshots`) got RLS enabled with **no** select policy — default-deny for `anon`/`authenticated`, service role bypasses as always — since nothing in Volume 2 indicates these are read directly by the frontend via Realtime the way `recommendations` status updates are; they're consumed through the API Gateway. Unlike §4's public sports data (Milestone 2), these are not made public.
  - **Schema correctness:** verified via `mcp__Supabase__list_tables` — all 7 tables present with correct columns/types/constraints/FKs, `recommendation_agent_outputs.id` confirmed defaulting to `uuid_generate_v7()`, all 7 `rls_enabled: true` (38 tables total across all three milestones).
  - **Append-only trigger, verified by a real `UPDATE` attempt, not just checking it exists:** used the exact function/trigger names §11 specifies (`block_agent_output_updates()`, `trg_block_rao_update`) — an `UPDATE` on a seeded `recommendation_agent_outputs` row was wrapped in a `DO` block that re-raises unless the caught error matches the append-only exception text; passed silently (blocked as expected).
  - **Tier-gated + soft-delete RLS, verified with a real cross-tier read test, not just policy existence:** seeded one free-tier user with an active free subscription, plus three `recommendations` rows — free/active, elite/active, and free/soft-deleted. Switched to `role authenticated` with that user's `request.jwt.claims` and queried all three: the free/active row returned **1** row, the elite/active row returned **0** (tier gating working), and the free/soft-deleted row also returned **0** (soft-delete filter working even though the tier matched).
  - **Default-deny verified for the six non-`recommendations` tables, not assumed from the missing policy:** in the same authenticated-role test, `agents`, `recommendation_agent_outputs`, `consensus_snapshots`, `explainability_payloads`, `recommendation_snapshots`, and `agent_performance_scores` all returned **0** rows despite each having a seeded row present — confirming RLS-enabled-with-no-policy actually denies reads rather than merely being configured that way.
  - **Dev confirmed unpolluted:** all test data (one `auth.users` row, `user_profiles`, `subscriptions`, `games`, 3× `recommendations`, `agents`, `recommendation_agent_outputs`) was inserted inside `begin`/`rollback`; a follow-up `execute_sql` call independently confirmed everything is back to 0 rows.
  - `staging`/`production` untouched, per Volume 3 §12's dev-first promotion path.
- **2026-08-07 — Milestone 4 (performance attribution, postgame review, market monitoring, AI orchestration config — Volume 3 §6–§8) scoped table-by-table, applied to `dev`, and verified.** Mac's explicit instruction going in: don't assume Milestone 3's default-deny pattern carries over — decide and state the RLS treatment for every new table individually. Result, decided before writing SQL:
  - **Settled directly by existing blueprint text (§10 names these explicitly in its `auth.uid() = user_id` list):** `bet_slips`, `verified_bets`, `projected_user_performance`, `verified_user_performance`.
  - **The one table that does NOT default-deny:** `feature_flags` — §4's own text says it's "consumed by... the frontend (beta dashboard features)," an explicit statement of frontend access unlike anything said about Milestone 3's internal tables. Given public-read, same pattern as Milestone 2.
  - **No textual signal either way — defaulted to Milestone 3's deny-by-default pattern, flagged rather than assumed:** `ai_performance`, `postgame_reviews`, `market_monitoring_events`, `model_routing_rules`, `prompt_registry`, `model_registry`, `recommendation_costs`, `audit_log`. Explicitly called out `market_monitoring_events` as the least certain of these (Volume 2 mentions Realtime events like `RecommendationWithdrawn`, but those more plausibly ride on `recommendations.status` changes — which already has its own RLS — rather than requiring this table itself to be open); can revisit if Phase 3/5 build-out proves otherwise.
  - Migration: `supabase/migrations/20260807220949_performance_postgame_config_tables.sql` — 13 tables total, applied cleanly on top of Milestones 1–3 plus the UUIDv7 fix.
  - **`market_monitoring_events` on `uuid_generate_v7()`** per the v2.0 amendment (third and final of the three named tables — `odds_snapshots`, `recommendation_agent_outputs`, now this one) — confirmed via `information_schema.columns`.
  - **The third §11 trigger, added now for the first time since its dependent tables (`audit_log`, `prompt_registry`, `model_routing_rules`, `feature_flags`) didn't exist before this milestone:** `log_config_change()`, attached to `prompt_registry`, `agents` (table existed since Milestone 3, trigger added now), `model_routing_rules`, and `feature_flags`. - **2026-08-09 — Two environment constraints discovered empirically and flagged to Mac before proceeding, not silently worked around:**
  1. **This session's sandbox cannot reach Supabase's Auth REST API or any Railway-deployed service's public URL.** Confirmed via the outbound proxy's own status endpoint: both `nhwjtsdebgiwskshzqiq.supabase.co` and `api.railway.app` return a policy-level 403 on CONNECT (`"gateway answered 403 to CONNECT (policy denial or upstream failure)"`), and the proxy's own README explicitly says to report rather than route around such denials. There is also no Supabase MCP tool exposing the Auth JWT signing secret, so locally-signed test tokens matching Supabase's real signing scheme aren't possible either. **Resolution:** GitHub Actions runners have unrestricted internet access: local pytest suites (this sandbox) cover all of the Gateway/internal-token code's own logic using mocked HTTP responses (respx), and a CI-based job will perform the genuinely-live signup/login/cross-environment calls against real deployed URLs, with results read back via the GitHub Actions API/logs — real evidence, executed in a different environment than this one.
  2. **Design consequence: the Gateway delegates JWT validation entirely to Supabase Auth's own `/auth/v1/user` endpoint** rather than decoding/verifying tokens itself (which would have required either the raw HS256 secret, unavailable, or JWKS-based verification of unconfirmed applicability to this project's actual signing scheme). This also means there's no separate local "expiry-checking" code path to unit-test in isolation — an expired token and a tampered/malformed token hit the identical code path (Supabase rejects it, Gateway returns 401), already proven locally (`test_tampered_or_invalid_token_rejected_by_supabase_returns_401`, passing). The specific "naturally expired real token" case can only be proven live, and even then only by actually waiting out Supabase's real token lifetime (commonly ~1hr) — not fabricated. Flagged plainly rather than claimed as tested when it wasn't.
- **2026-08-09 — Milestone 1: signup trigger built, applied to dev, and verified with real inserts (not just reviewed as SQL).** `handle_new_user()` (SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path = public`) inserts into `user_profiles` on every `auth.users` INSERT, `on conflict (id) do nothing` for idempotency. Verified in a rolled-back transaction: a real `auth.users` insert produced exactly 1 `user_profiles` row with `jurisdiction_state` correctly `null` (not yet collected); a simulated duplicate-trigger-fire (re-inserting the same id with the same `on conflict` clause) left exactly 1 row, no error. Dev confirmed back to exactly the 2 intentional seed users afterward.
  - **Blueprint-vs-reality conflict found and resolved, not improvised around:** `jurisdiction_state` was `not null` (Volume 3 §3), but jurisdiction is collected at onboarding (Milestone 4), after signup — the trigger has no jurisdiction value at the moment it fires. Relaxed to nullable; intent enforced at the application layer instead, exactly as Phase 2's own Key Tasks anticipated. CHANGELOG v4.2 (MINOR, Volume 3 only); Volume 3 §3 body and header updated in the same pass, not header-only.
  - **Unrelated Phase 1 defect found and fixed under Mac's proven-defect carve-out:** Supabase's own security advisor (checked opportunistically while starting Milestone 1) flagged 4 Phase 1 functions (`block_snapshot_updates`, `uuid_generate_v7`, `block_agent_output_updates`, `log_config_change`) with mutable `search_path` — a real search_path-hijacking risk, not a Phase 2 architectural choice. Fixed via `alter function ... set search_path = public` on all 4; `set_updated_at` was not flagged and was left untouched. Also surfaced: "leaked password protection" is disabled in Supabase Auth — flagged as a recommended hardening item, but no MCP tool exposes Auth config settings to change it; needs Mac's action in the Supabase dashboard if he wants it enabled.
- **2026-08-09 — Milestone 2: Gateway JWT validation built, local tests passing (12/12, `apps/api-gateway`), live deployment/verification pending.** `apps/api-gateway/app/auth.py`'s `get_current_user` dependency: extracts the Bearer token, calls Supabase Auth's `/auth/v1/user` to validate it (any non-200 → 401, network errors → 401 not 500), then confirms a `user_profiles` row still exists for that user id via PostgREST with the service role key (defense-in-depth for the deleted-user case, since a JWT alone doesn't know its user was deleted). `GET /v1/user/profile` (Volume 2 §6's own endpoint list) is the protected route used for testing. Local suite (`respx`-mocked, no live network) covers: missing header, malformed header, empty bearer token, Supabase-rejected token, Supabase network error, deleted-user token, and the happy path — all passing, run for real in this sandbox (`python -m pytest`, matching CI's exact invocation), not asserted without running.
- **2026-08-09 — Milestone 3: internal service token built, local tests passing (6/6, `apps/ai-orchestrator`), Railway config applied and independently verified, live cross-environment testing pending.** `apps/ai-orchestrator/app/internal_auth.py`'s `require_internal_token` dependency guards a new `GET /v1/internal/ping` endpoint, constant-time comparison (`hmac.compare_digest`) against `INTERNAL_SERVICE_TOKEN`. Three distinct 64-hex-char tokens generated (`secrets.token_hex(32)`), one per environment, set on `api-gateway`/`ai-orchestrator`/`sports-intel-layer`/`worker-scheduled`/`worker-market-monitor` (15 Railway calls, `skipDeploys: true`) — independently verified via `list-variables` (spot-checked dev/api-gateway and production/worker-market-monitor) and via `get-status` on production confirming deployment timestamps unchanged (no accidental redeploy, same discipline as every prior Railway mutation this project). Token values were never echoed back after being set. Rotation procedure documented in `docs/ops/secrets-management.md` per Mac's explicit instruction, even though automated rotation is deferred.

A valid submission updates `user_profiles` via PostgREST and sets `onboarding_completed_at`. Local tests cover: missing jurisdiction → 422, blank jurisdiction → 422, valid submission → 200 with `onboarding_completed_at` populated, and unauthenticated request → 401.
- **2026-08-10 — Live E2E verification run for real, via GitHub Actions (`scripts/phase2_e2e_test.py`, `.github/workflows/phase2-e2e.yml`).** Multiple real defects and environment gaps were found and fixed along the way, not smoothed over:
  1. **Repo default branch was a stale session branch, not `main`.** `workflow_dispatch` requires the target workflow file to exist on GitHub's actual default branch, and the repo's `default_branch` was `claude/new-session-6rapus` — unrelated to the project's real `dev`→`main`→tag branch strategy. Flagged to Mac; he corrected it via GitHub's own settings (no MCP tool exposes this).
  2. **Real bug: `api-gateway` was missing `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` in all three Railway environments.** Phase 1 only ever set it on `frontend`; Phase 2's `get_current_user` needs it to call Supabase's `/auth/v1/user`. Every authenticated request was throwing an unhandled `KeyError` → 500 instead of the correct 401 for a bad token. Fixed by setting it on `api-gateway` in dev/staging/production (verified via `list-variables`, dev redeployed and reconfirmed); `secrets-management.md`'s inventory corrected to match. Confirmed via grep that no other service touches Supabase Auth, so no other service needed it.
  3. **Test-script bug, not a product bug:** `@example.com` test emails were rejected by Supabase itself (RFC 2606 reserved domain) — fixed by switching the test domain.
  4. **Real environment gap: no custom SMTP was ever configured**, so Supabase's default email sender's very low rate limit (2/hour) blocked live signup testing. Mac set up Resend, initially using the shared `onboarding@resend.dev` sender — which turned out to only deliver to the Resend account owner's own verified address (confirmed via Supabase's own auth logs, not assumed), so a Gmail `+alias` workaround was tried and also failed (Resend does an exact match, not alias-aware). Mac then purchased and verified `playbookai.cc` in Resend (subdomain `mail.playbookai.cc`, Cloudflare-integrated DKIM), and signup emails route through that for real as of this entry.
  5. **Cross-environment scenarios 7/8 against `production` correctly report `NOT_YET_TESTABLE`, not FAIL** — production has no Phase 2 deployment yet (release-tag gated, same Phase 0 constraint), so a 404 there isn't a security gap, just an untested pair. The script was fixed to distinguish this from a real 401-mismatch.
  6. **Real product behavior confirmed, not a defect:** Supabase's dev project requires email confirmation before login. Test accounts can't click their own confirmation emails, so scenarios 5, 10, and the "token still works" half of scenario 4 needed a real session token. Added a `finish_scenarios` mode that logs into accounts confirmed out-of-band (directly against `auth.users`, always scoped by both exact id AND the throwaway `@playbook-e2e-test.com` email pattern together — never a bare match, per Mac's explicit safety requirement) — and confirmed via SQL that each UPDATE/DELETE affected exactly the expected row count before proceeding.
  7. **Scenario 4's access token never left the GitHub Actions process.** The first attempt tried relaying the token back through retrieved job logs into a second run, but the token came back redacted (credential-shaped content). Redesigned so the same job polls the protected endpoint with the same token every 5s for up to 120s while the underlying row is deleted out-of-band in parallel — a stronger proof than the original two-run design, and the token is never exposed to me or the conversation.
  - **Final result: 8 of 10 scenarios independently PASSed with real evidence; 2 (production-side cross-environment token checks) are NOT_YET_TESTABLE pending Mac's release tag.** Full scenario-by-scenario table presented to Mac for review.
- **2026-08-10 — Mac's explicit instruction: do not close Phase 2 on this evidence alone.** Current state recorded as Phase 2 implementation COMPLETE, acceptance verification PENDING FINAL PRODUCTION CHECKS — deliberately not weakening the phase-gating standard just to move forward. Two follow-ups required before a final acceptance report:
  1. **Scenario 2 (expired JWT) — investigated whether a live test could be done safely and deterministically, per Mac's explicit condition, without waiting out a real ~1hr expiry (which he ruled out as manufacturing evidence) or weakening security.** Considered: signing out the token's session via Supabase's own `signOut()` and re-testing the same access token, expecting rejection. Checked against Supabase's own docs before running it (not assumed) — confirmed this would NOT have worked: "There is no way to revoke a user's access token jwt until it expires. It is recommended to set a shorter expiry on the jwt for this reason" (Supabase JS reference, `auth-signout`). The only other paths — forging a token with a past `exp` (requires the JWT signing secret, which the Gateway's whole design deliberately doesn't have) or shortening the global JWT expiry setting to force a fast real expiry (alters real security behavior for all users solely to manufacture a test condition) — are both excluded by Mac's stated conditions. **Conclusion: no safe/deterministic live test is possible under the stated constraints. Scenario 2 remains on local automated evidence only** (`test_tampered_or_invalid_token_rejected_by_supabase_returns_401`, passing) — documented as a stated limitation, not silently skipped.
  2. **Scenarios 7/8's production-side rows remain NOT_YET_TESTABLE.** Per Mac's explicit instruction, a 404 (no deployment) does not count as authentication-rejection evidence — these must be re-run for real once Phase 2 reaches production through the normal release-tag path (the same tag-protection constraint since Phase 0; only Mac pushes release tags). Once deployed, three checks will be run: dev's internal token → production (expect 401), staging's internal token → production (expect 401), and production's own internal token → production (expect 200, positive-proof sanity check). No manual production changes will be made to force this — it waits on Mac's normal deploy path. **Phase 2 remains open pending these production checks and Mac's review of the resulting final acceptance report.**

Also surfaced in the same pass: the model's docstring claimed to mirror `daily_game_intelligence`'s `confidence` field, which doesn't actually exist on `AdapterResponse` — an over-claim, not a real gap, but worth catching and fixing rather than leaving stated and unimplemented.

(2) Explicitly declined to add a generic `confidence` field — The Playbook's own confidence is a downstream-derived value (feeding the AI Transparency Meter's `data_quality` dimension) and must never be silently conflated with a provider-supplied confidence value; if a specific provider later exposes one worth preserving, that's handled deliberately as provider-supplied metadata, not folded into this field by default. (3) Docstring corrected to state the envelope's actual scope (provenance metadata only) rather than the prior over-claim. (4) `status`/freshness computation deliberately left unimplemented in 3A and still assigned to Milestone E (Master Refresh + scheduled workers), which has the cadence table and call history a single stateless adapter fetch can't have on its own — not pulled forward just to make the contract look more complete.
  - **5 new tests** (`tests/adapters/test_provenance.py`): `provider_reported_at` survives normalization when supplied, stays null when not supplied, is independently generated from `fetched_at` (a 6-hour-old supplied provider timestamp doesn't leak into `fetched_at`, verified with an explicit assertion, not just "different enough"), survives a full JSON cache round-trip, and the vendor-swap acceptance criterion still holds after the change. **12/12 tests passing — verified locally, then independently confirmed passing in CI (`test-python-services (sports-intel-layer)`) before merging, not assumed identical.**

Stated assumptions (none of these are blueprint-given, all flagged for Mac to confirm/adjust): 1 region (`us`), 3 markets for bulk odds (h2h/spreads/totals, matching `odds_snapshots.market_type`), 5 representative player-prop markets, ~13 games on a peak Sunday slate.
  - **Literal 24/7 five-minute cadence, as currently specified:** low (no games) day ≈ 867 credits; normal (1-game weekday) day ≈ 2,317 credits; peak (Sunday slate) day ≈ **19,717 credits — nearly the entire 20K/mo tier consumed in a single day**, driven almost entirely by Player Props Worker's 5-minute cadence across ~13 concurrent games, 24 hours a day. Projected in-season month (≈4 peak Sundays + 8 normal game days + 18 off days): **≈113,000 credits/month**, exceeding the 100K/$59 tier and requiring the 5M/$119 tier.
  - **Real cost lever found, not yet a decision:** if Player Props Worker only actively polls during a realistic game-hours window (~12h on game days, idle on off days) rather than literally 24/7/365, projected usage drops to **≈70,000 credits/month**, fitting the 100K/$59 tier with headroom. This is a worker-scheduling design choice that hasn't been made yet, not a props/regions/markets trade-off — flagged for Mac's decision rather than assumed either way.
  - **Environment-isolation cost implication also flagged:** Volume 2 §5's staging-uses-real-APIs policy implies staging needs its own paid subscription, separate from production's (matching the existing internal-token/Redis isolation principle) — meaning whichever tier is chosen is potentially needed twice unless staging intentionally runs a reduced cadence/scope, which is a legitimate option since the policy requires real data, not identical volume to production.

The gap is downstream, in persistence: (1) `daily_game_intelligence`'s existing v4.0 per-category jsonb convention already carries `source` and `last_updated` (maps to `fetched_at`) but has no slot for `provider_reported_at` or `from_cache` — a schema-free fix (add keys to the existing jsonb shape) when Milestone F actually builds the write path, not a migration. (2) The bigger gap: the append-only snapshot tables that are the *actual* permanent historical record per §1's Time Machine pattern — `odds_snapshots`, `injury_reports`, `weather_snapshots`, `depth_chart_snapshots`, `referee_assignments`, `team_stats`, `player_stats` — carry **no provenance columns at all**, only `captured_at`/`created_at` (our own insert time, not the provider's). A worker persisting a row into any of these today has nowhere to put the adapter's `source`/`provider_reported_at`/`from_cache` even if it wanted to. **Proposed minimal migration (not applied — per Mac's explicit instruction to map first, not auto-add columns):** add three columns, same names/types, to all seven tables above — `source text`, `provider_reported_at timestamptz`, `from_cache boolean not null default false` (nullability/backfill approach for existing seed rows to be finalized when Milestone F actually implements this, following the same not-null-relaxation precedent already set by the v4.2 `jurisdiction_state` decision). - **2026-08-10 — Corrected a real factual error surfaced by Mac's own review: finalized NFL stats are not permanently immutable at initial ingestion.** Researched (WebSearch, primary vendor site still egress-blocked) and confirmed via multiple converging fantasy-platform sources (ESPN, Yahoo, CBS, Sleeper, NFL.com) plus SportsDataIO's own published NFL Data Workflow Guide: the NFL/Elias Sports Bureau issues official stat corrections for up to ~7 days post-game, concentrated Wednesday–Thursday. SportsDataIO exposes an `IsClosed` boolean (recommended over bare `Status == Final` specifically for bet-resulting) and a `LastUpdated` revision timestamp — both directly usable as correction indicators. SportsDataIO's own guidance: refresh completed-game boxscores every 10min–2h for the first 24h, tracked 48–72h for full safety. **Design proposed (pending Mac's confirmation, not yet built):** `GameFinished` still triggers the initial ingestion as before, followed by a small, fixed number of bounded reconciliation checks (not continuous polling) spanning the confirmed real-world correction window, using the newly-proposed `provider_reported_at`/`source` columns to detect when a fetched `LastUpdated` is newer than what's stored. Corrections persist as a **new row**, following the exact append-only precedent already set by `odds_snapshots` — `team_stats`/`player_stats` already have no uniqueness constraint blocking multiple rows per player/team+game, so this requires no new column, only the provenance columns already proposed above. Volume 2 §8's new Postgame Ingestion Worker row and the CHANGELOG v4.2 entry were both corrected in the same pass to stop claiming a completed game's stats are fetched "exactly once"/immutable, so the Blueprint doesn't ship an overclaim. **Open, unresolved, and explicitly flagged for Mac:** how a stat correction should interact with bet grading if a bet was already graded against the pre-correction value — this is a product/architecture decision, not something to decide silently.
- **2026-08-10 — Mac approved the full 6-rule Stat Correction ↔ Bet Settlement Policy, closing the open item above. Documented in Volume 3 §6 (not §7 — corrected from the initial imprecise citation before writing anything, since `verified_bets`, the table this policy is actually about, lives in §6; §7's `postgame_reviews` got a one-line cross-reference instead of the primary text) via the standard CHANGELOG.md four-field discipline, bumping Volume 3 to v4.3 (MINOR).** Policy: sportsbook settlement and sports-stat truth are separate records, never automatically equivalent; a wager's graded outcome comes from the official settlement source, never inferred from corrected stats; a stat correction preserves history, records that it occurred, and never silently overwrites a settled outcome; a sportsbook regrade preserves the original settlement and records the regrade as a new event, not an in-place update; performance analytics use the effective settled outcome, never the stats tables directly; Time Machine reconstruction must distinguish recommendation-time info, initial postgame stats, later corrections, the original settlement, any regrade, and the current effective outcome. **Explicitly Phase 5 policy — no bet-grading logic implemented in Phase 3.** Schema gap check performed before writing (per Mac's instruction): rules 1/2/5 already structurally satisfied (no coupling exists between `verified_bets` and the stats tables); rule 3's correction-marker need folded into the already-proposed, not-yet-applied Milestone F provenance migration; **rule 4 surfaced a real, unclosed gap — `verified_bets` has no append-only/versioning pattern (unlike `odds_snapshots`), so an in-place regrade update today would destructively overwrite the original settlement.** Per Mac's explicit instruction not to pull Phase 5 implementation backward into Phase 3, this gap is documented and flagged for Phase 5's schema work (likely an `odds_snapshots`-style settlement-history table), **not closed now** — no migration applied, no new table created. Confirmed Phase 3's current design (Postgame Ingestion Worker inserts new rows, never touches `verified_bets`) already satisfies the "don't foreclose this policy" boundary Mac set — no Phase 3 architecture change required.

Grounding check before building anything: this isn't a new architectural compromise — Volume 2 §5 already specifies "Development: Sandbox APIs where the provider offers them, fake/seeded data otherwise," and Phase 3's own Acceptance Criteria text already reads "Real **(or sandboxed)** provider data flows end-to-end" — fixtures are the blueprint-sanctioned Development-environment path, not an invented shortcut. - **2026-08-12 — Phase 3C-ii implementation approved and built: five concrete SportsDataIO adapters (PR #41, merged).** Before writing code, verified compatibility between Mac's explicit architecture decision (keep The Playbook's internal contract per-game, don't leak SportsDataIO's week-scoped bulk API shape upward) and the existing 3A adapter contracts / 3B-3C-i cache architecture — no conflict found, no interface change needed, since `TeamStatsAdapter.fetch_team_stats`/`PlayerStatsAdapter.fetch_player_stats` were already per-game at the 3A ABC level. Built:
  - `SportsDataIORosterAdapter` — merges `/Players/{team}` + `/DepthCharts` into one `RosterEntry` stream; `DepthOrder` sourced from DepthCharts only (proven in the fixture itself: Worthy's own Players-endpoint `DepthOrder` is 3, DepthCharts says 1 — the merge uses 1). A DepthCharts failure propagates rather than silently falling back to Players' own unreliable depth fields, since no adapter in this codebase has an established partial-success pattern.
  - `SportsDataIOScheduleAdapter` — `DateTimeUTC`→`scheduled_start`, `StadiumDetails.Name`→`stadium`, `Status`→`status` (only `"Scheduled"` mapped; anything else raises rather than guessing).
  - `SportsDataIOTeamStatsAdapter` / `SportsDataIOPlayerStatsAdapter` — a second, adapter-internal cache layer (reusing the existing `CacheBackend`/`cache_key` primitives from `app/adapters/cache.py`, not a new subsystem) holds the raw week-bulk payload; a second game from an already-cached week triggers no second provider call, proven by a respx `call_count == 1` test. `PlayerStatLine.stats` preserves the nested `ScoringDetails` sub-structure (a genuinely new shape, matching SportsDataIO's own documented "Included data tables: PlayerGame, ScoringDetail").
  - `SportsDataIOInjuryAdapter` — `InjuryReport.game_external_id` derived via an injected `game_key_for(team, opponent, season, week)` resolver (same pattern as `WeatherAPIWeatherAdapter.location_for_game`, 3C-i), since the Injuries response carries no game identifier at all; a resolver returning `None` (bye week) skips that record rather than raising. `InjuryReport.description` deliberately left `None` — checked `models.py`/`base.py`/the blueprint volumes for its intended semantics first, found no field-level spec anywhere, and the three scrambled candidate fields (`BodyPart`/`Practice`/`PracticeDescription`) can't be disambiguated by content. - **2026-08-13 — Phase 3D: `RedisCacheBackend` implemented and tested, no hosted Redis provisioned (PR #43, merged).** `RedisCacheBackend(CacheBackend)` added to `app/adapters/cache.py`, dependency-injected with a `redis.asyncio.Redis`-compatible client — same pattern every provider adapter already uses for its `httpx.AsyncClient`. `CacheBackend`, `CachingAdapter`, and `cache_key()` are byte-for-byte unchanged; no incompatibility surfaced. Pure string store (Redis handles bytes natively, only `bytes.decode()` on read — not a new serialization format); native Redis `EX` expiry, no TTL logic reimplemented; connection/timeout failures **fail open** (cache miss on `get`, swallowed no-op on `set`) rather than raised, a deliberate decision — a struggling cache must not take down the system it protects. Category-appropriate TTL support required no special-casing in the backend itself, since `ttl_seconds` was already a per-call argument in the existing interface — added `CATEGORY_TTL_SECONDS`, a documented, ASSUMED-tagged starting-point mapping (CONFIRMED FROM VOLUME 2 §8 for Weather/News's flat 15-minute cadence; ASSUMED simplifications elsewhere, explicitly flagged where a category's real cadence is adaptive/window-aware rather than a flat number) for 3E's future wiring to use.

- **2026-08-14 — Phase 3E-4: Odds Worker + Player Props Worker implemented, tested, documented** (approved after Mac accepted 3E-3, full 3E-4A–G checkpoint issued in one message).
  - **3E-4A — team-identity coverage:** `teams` expanded 6 → 32 (all current NFL teams; standard public naming, not provider-specific data) via `20260814050000_expand_nfl_teams_and_provider_ids.sql`. `team_provider_ids` sportsdataio coverage expanded 4 → 13 of 32 (9 new mappings — ARI, ATL, CAR, CHI, LAR, NE, NO, SEA, TB — each confirmed by direct grep of this repo's own fixtures before writing the migration). `the_odds_api` coverage unchanged at 6 of 32. **Reported per Mac's explicit "STOP and report" instruction:** 19 teams have zero SportsDataIO fixture evidence, 26 have zero The Odds API fixture evidence — not filled in from general knowledge. **Retroactive provenance finding, disclosed not silently fixed:** auditing against the stricter fixture-only bar this checkpoint set surfaced that 2 of 3E-3's six `TEAM_BACKFILL` entries (Dallas Cowboys/Philadelphia Eagles → sportsdataio `"DAL"`/`"PHI"`) were never actually fixture-confirmed — removed from `TEAM_BACKFILL` (the Python source of truth) going forward; their already-applied 3E-3 database rows deliberately left in place rather than unilaterally rewritten, flagged here for Mac's review.
  - **3E-4B — model contract:** `OddsLine`/`PlayerProp` widened with `home_team`/`away_team`/`commence_time` — the first two per Mac's own named gap from the 3E-3 audit, the third (`commence_time`) a real requirement direct inspection surfaced while building 3E-4C (needed for scheduled-start validation), beyond what was originally named. `TheOddsApiOddsAdapter`/`TheOddsApiPlayerPropsAdapter` updated to populate all three from the provider's own event payload. `fetch_odds([])` gained discovery-mode behavior (returns every event unfiltered) — a necessary adapter-behavior change, not scope creep: the bulk endpoint already returns the full slate regardless of filter at no extra cost, and there is no other way for a worker to learn a not-yet-linked game's own The Odds API event id.
  - **3E-4C — deterministic game linking, promoted to production:** `app/persistence/odds_game_linking.py`, built from the 3E-3 audit's proven mechanism. Pipeline exactly as Mac specified: event → home/away identities → `team_provider_ids` → `teams.id` → SportsDataIO team identities → candidate game (±1 day window) → scheduled-start validation (6-hour tolerance — an ASSUMED, documented judgment call, no Blueprint number exists for this) → `games.id`. Every named failure mode has a dedicated passing test: unknown team, missing mapping, zero matches, multiple matches (ambiguous, unresolved), reversed home/away (naturally handled by strict ordered matching, never a false match), kickoff-time mismatch, rescheduled game, and lookup/malformed-event isolation in the batch entry point. Unresolved events are logged (WARNING, mirroring `app.adapters.cache`'s existing logging convention — the only precedent in this codebase) and returned to the caller, never silently dropped.
  - **3E-4D/E — the two workers:** `app/workers/odds_worker.py` and `app/workers/player_props_worker.py`. Both: read candidate games via the existing `list_games_in_window`, classify by kickoff proximity, skip anything not due, isolate per-event/per-game failures, append-only persistence, zero calls to Master Refresh (proven structurally, not just claimed — a test asserts `app/master_refresh/run.py`'s own source never mentions `the_odds_api`/`TheOddsApiOddsAdapter`/player_props). **Real structural difference between the two, not a design preference:** Odds Worker self-discovers and links not-yet-linked games via the bulk endpoint's discovery mode; Player Props Worker cannot (its endpoint is event-specific, requires an already-known id) and skips a due-but-unlinked game for that cycle instead — flagged as an assumption for Mac to confirm (Player Props depends on linkage established elsewhere, to avoid duplicating a cost-relevant discovery call between two independently-cadenced workers). Player Props persists via new `persist_player_props` (`app/persistence/odds_snapshots.py`), `market_type='prop'` — confirmed against Volume 3 §4's own schema comment before implementing, no conflict, no new column/table.
  - **3E-4F — dynamic TTL (Decision 4):** implemented via the same shared policy as cadence (`app.workers.windows.ttl_seconds`) — never a second, independent timing rule. `app/adapters/cache.py`'s flat `CATEGORY_TTL_SECONDS["odds"]`/`["player_props"]` are now documented as superseded for these two workers (dead weight for any other flat-TTL caller, of which none exists). Injury dynamic TTL explicitly deferred to 3E-5 per Mac's instruction; `app.workers.windows` is written generically enough to be reused there without modification.
  - **3E-4G — timezone/DST correctness:** every game comparison is timezone-aware; a naive datetime raises `ValueError` immediately rather than being silently treated as UTC or the server's local time. Tests cover all four continental US timezones, Sunday early/late windows, SNF/MNF/TNF, a UTC-calendar-boundary-crossing kickoff, and both 2026 DST transitions (spring-forward March 8, fall-back November 1). **A real bug found and fixed during this phase's own testing, not merely tested for:** two aware datetimes sharing the same `zoneinfo.ZoneInfo` tzinfo object straddling a DST transition silently subtract via CPython's naive wall-clock fallback (a documented stdlib behavior, not a bug in Python itself) rather than the true elapsed time — `classify_window`/`should_poll` now always normalize both operands through UTC before subtracting, closing this exact gap; a regression test proves the corrected arithmetic against the naive (wrong) result directly.
