- **2026-08-18 — Phase 3E-6: Weather Worker implemented, tested, documented** (approved after Mac chose Option A on the venue-data schema blocker presented during planning).
  - **The blocker, presented and resolved before any code was written:** inspection confirmed SportsDataIO's Schedule response genuinely includes `StadiumDetails.GeoLat`/`.GeoLong`/`.Type` (CONFIRMED in `tests/fixtures/sportsdataio/schedules_normal.json`), but `SportsDataIOScheduleAdapter.fetch_schedule` discarded all three during normalization, and `games` had nowhere to hold them — the identical gap Volume 3's v4.5 travel-semantics note already flagged for coordinates alone, now blocking a different consumer (weather location + dome detection). Three options presented (widen `games` directly / a dedicated `stadiums` reference table / defer this phase entirely and use `games.stadium` name as location with dome detection skipped) — **Mac approved Option A.**
  - **Schema (Option A):** `20260818050000_games_venue_fields.sql` — `games.venue_lat double precision`, `.venue_long double precision`, `.venue_type text check (in ('outdoor','dome','retractable_dome'))`, all nullable, applied live to dev Supabase and verified (`information_schema.columns` confirms all three, correct types, nullable). `ScheduleEntry` widened to carry the same three fields, scoped to exactly what Weather Worker needs — no other `StadiumDetails` fields (capacity, surface, city/state/country) carried.
  - **`_VENUE_TYPE_MAP` (new, `sportsdataio.py`), CONFIRMED FROM LIVE FREE TRIAL:** exactly three distinct raw `StadiumDetails.Type` values observed across this repo's own already-captured fixtures (`schedules_normal.json`, `teams_active_normal.json`) — `"Outdoor"`, `"Dome"`, `"RetractableDome"` — mapped 1:1 to `outdoor`/`dome`/`retractable_dome`. Same discipline as `_SEASON_TYPE_MAP`/`_SCHEDULE_STATUS_MAP`: a present-but-unrecognized value raises `ProviderDataError` rather than being silently passed through; a genuinely missing `StadiumDetails`/`Type` is a legitimate `None`, not an error.
  - **`WeatherConditions.is_dome` changed from `bool = False` to `bool | None = None`** — closes a real, pre-existing null-not-neutral gap in the 3C-i model (it could never represent "unknown" before this phase; every caller silently got "outdoor"). `WeatherAPIWeatherAdapter` now always returns `is_dome=None` (no vendor knows this) rather than hardcoding `False`; the worker overlays the real, venue-derived value before persisting.
  - **`venue_is_dome(venue_type) -> bool | None`:** `'dome'` → `True` (skip WeatherAPI entirely — proven by a test asserting zero forecast calls), `'outdoor'` → `False` (poll normally), `'retractable_dome'` or missing → `None` (genuinely unknown roof state — polling still happens if coordinates exist, since regional weather is still useful context, but the persisted `is_dome` stays `None`, never coerced to `False` — proven by two dedicated tests, one per case).
  - **Location resolution:** `"{venue_lat},{venue_long}"`, WeatherAPI's own documented `q`-parameter form — no geocoding vendor, no hardcoded stadium list. A game missing either coordinate is skipped (`games_skipped_unresolved_location`, `status="partial"` — a real gap, unlike a dome skip which stays `"success"`), never guessed.
  - **Cadence — flat, no tiers invented, per Mac's explicit instruction:** `_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 900`, verified to already match `CATEGORY_TTL_SECONDS["weather"]` exactly before reusing it (not assumed). Two boundaries DERIVED from existing convention rather than invented: the 7-day candidate window (same scoping every other worker independently adopted) and stop-at-kickoff (reusing `classify_window`'s existing `Window.STOPPED` directly — never its ramp-tier intervals, and `should_poll` itself was deliberately NOT reused wholesale since it would have silently applied Odds/Player Props' ramping intervals instead of a flat 900s).
  - **Identity — no `game_provider_ids` hop, a genuine architectural asymmetry checked before assuming symmetry with Odds/Player Props/Injury:** WeatherAPI has no native game/event concept at all; `game_provider_ids.provider_name`'s own check constraint doesn't even permit `'weatherapi'`. `WeatherConditions.game_external_id` is this project's own internal `games.id`, supplied directly by the worker — `app/persistence/weather_snapshots.py` deliberately does not call `resolve_game_ids`, unlike its sibling snapshot-persistence modules (documented explicitly in both modules' docstrings so a future session doesn't "fix" this into a bug).
  - **`app/workers/weather_worker.py`:** per-game fetch (matching Player Props' per-game isolation reasoning, since WeatherAPI's endpoint is per-game like theirs, not bulk like Injury's) — one game's provider failure or malformed response never blocks another's, proven by two dedicated isolation tests.
  - **`daily_game_intelligence` integration deliberately untouched:** `app.persistence.snapshots.latest_weather_snapshot` and `daily_game_intelligence.py`'s `weather` field assembly were both already built in 3E-2, speculatively ahead of this worker existing, and both already correctly read whatever `weather_snapshots` holds — proven by a downstream round-trip read-back test rather than assumed.
  - **24 new tests, 307/307 passing** (283 pre-existing + 24 new): `test_weather_worker.py` (21, new file — outdoor refresh, multiple games, dome skip, retractable/missing-type unknown-not-false, missing coordinates, no-candidates/STOPPED/not-due/15-min-boundary cadence, provider failure + malformed-response isolation, cache hit/stale, append-only rerun, downstream read-back, a DST-crossing cadence check reusing 3E-4G/3E-5's own spring-forward scenario), `test_sportsdataio_adapters.py` (+3: venue fields preserved/missing-stays-null/unrecognized-type-raises), `test_schedule_persistence.py` (+2: venue fields written/stay-null), `test_weatherapi_weather_adapter.py` (1 updated: `is_dome` assertion `False`→`None`). No live SportsDataIO or WeatherAPI calls anywhere in the build or test suite (SportsDataIO budget unchanged at 11 of 12; WeatherAPI calls: 0); no hosted Redis provisioned; no Railway scheduler configured.

Volume 3 → v4.9 (MINOR — `games.venue_lat`/`.venue_long`/`.venue_type`, closes the exact gap v4.5's travel-semantics note deferred; that note updated to reflect the coordinate-storage foundation now exists, though `travel` itself remains unbuilt). - **2026-08-18 — Phase 3E-7: News Worker implemented, tested, documented** (both required blockers presented before any code was written; Mac proceeded on the presented recommendations — provider-neutral build, Option A persistence).
  - **Blocker 1 (provider decision), re-confirmed by direct inspection, not assumed from memory:** Volume 2 §8 still names NewsAPI default/GNews fallback; `PROGRESS.md`'s own history (2026-08-11) shows Mac explicitly held back the comparison ("pending a coverage/latency/reliability/licensing comparison"), never revisited. `find -iname "*gnews*"` across the repo: zero hits — no GNews code exists at all. Classified **BLOCKER**, not CONFIRMED. Resolution: built provider-neutrally against the existing `NewsAdapter`/`NewsArticle` interface (the same adapter-pattern guarantee every other category already relies on) — `NewsAPINewsAdapter` (3C-i) wired in only because it's the sole adapter that exists, not because the vendor question is settled. A future `GNewsNewsAdapter` requires zero change to `news_worker.py`.
  - **Blocker 2 (News-history persistence gap), re-confirmed by direct inspection of the CURRENT Volume 3 (not the pre-3E-6 version):** §4's snapshot-table list still names only `injury_reports`/`weather_snapshots`/`depth_chart_snapshots`/`referee_assignments` — `news` is not among them; `daily_game_intelligence.news jsonb` (line 315 as of this session) remains the only place News lives. Three options presented (A: current-state-only into `daily_game_intelligence.news`, no new table / B: append-only `news_snapshots`/`news_reports`, team-keyed since `NewsArticle` has no game identity / C: a lighter middle ground using the existing metadata envelope) with pros/cons/Time-Machine/reproducibility/schema-impact analysis. **Proceeded on Option A** (the presented recommendation) after Mac's go-ahead — no Phase 3 acceptance criterion requires News history, and Option B's correct shape (team-keyed) would be a genuine schema departure from every other snapshot table's game-keyed convention, inheriting the same vendor-ToS/commercial-storage-terms question the original Milestone F deferral was about. Option B remains available future work, gated on Blocker 1.
  - **A deliberate, explicitly-flagged departure from Weather's stop-at-kickoff convention:** `app/workers/news_worker.py` never imports `app.workers.windows` at all — news about a team (injury updates, postgame reactions, next-week previews) doesn't stop being relevant at that team's own kickoff, unlike pregame odds/weather/props where the underlying data itself becomes structurally meaningless once the game starts. The only boundary reused from existing convention is the 7-day candidate-game window itself (same `_CANDIDATE_WINDOW_DAYS` every other specialized worker independently adopted) — proven by a dedicated test (`test_news_worker_never_stops_at_kickoff`) showing a team whose game already kicked off is still polled.
  - **Polling unit is a resolved `teams.id`, not a game** — `last_polled_at` and the cache key both follow this, the natural extension of "each worker's polling entity matches its real fetch granularity" (Weather/Odds/Player Props: per-game; Injury: one bulk timestamp) to News's team-scoped `fetch_news(team=...)` contract.
  - **Identity resolution, zero fuzzy matching:** `games.home_team`/`.away_team` (SportsDataIO abbreviations, since Master Refresh only writes SportsDataIO-sourced games) resolve to `teams.id` via the existing `team_identity.resolve_team_ids` (`provider_name="sportsdataio"`, now 32/32-confirmed per the 2026-08-18 verification round), then to the canonical `teams.name` (`"Kansas City Chiefs"`) `fetch_news` actually expects via the new `app/persistence/teams.py::list_teams_by_id` — a lookup direction neither `team_identity.py` nor any other existing module performed. An abbreviation with no mapping is `teams_unresolved`, excluded from that cycle, never guessed (proven by `test_unmapped_abbreviation_is_unresolved_not_guessed` and `test_both_sides_unresolved_skips_the_game_entirely`).
  - **League-wide/unassigned news deliberately out of scope, not silently forced:** this worker only ever calls `fetch_news(team=<resolved name>)`, never the bare unscoped `fetch_news(team=None)` "NFL" query — that query's results have `related_teams=[]` (no trustworthy team relationship), and forcing them onto any specific game would be exactly the guessed relevance the Blueprint prohibits (proven by `test_never_calls_unscoped_news_query`, which asserts every real provider call is team-qualified). A real model/table-shape mismatch, flagged rather than papered over: true league-wide news has nowhere correct to live in the current schema.
  - **Player-level news confirmed structurally unsupported, not a gap this worker papers over:** `NewsArticle` has no player field at all (`test_news_article_model_has_no_player_field` asserts the model's exact field set) — only `related_teams`.
  - **Defensive response-side validation, not blind trust of the adapter:** `_validate_articles` confirms every returned article's own `related_teams` actually names the team it was queried for before attribution. `NewsAPINewsAdapter` already guarantees this by construction (unit-tested directly via `test_validate_articles_drops_articles_with_mismatched_related_teams`, since a real mismatch can't occur through the current adapter's own contract) — this guards against a future/different `NewsAdapter` implementation that doesn't preserve it.
  - **Failure/stale-data handling:** a team's failed fetch this cycle excludes it from that cycle's results; any game depending only on that team is skipped — `write_news` is never called, so whatever `news` already held stays untouched (proven by `test_provider_failure_preserves_last_known_good`: a second-cycle 503 leaves the first cycle's write as the only one). A team's fetch succeeding with zero articles **is** written (`value: []`, fresh `status`/`last_updated`) — a distinct, positive "checked, nothing" result (proven by `test_no_relevant_news_writes_fresh_empty_value`), never the same as "not re-checked."
  - **Malformed-article handling stays whole-request** — `NewsAPINewsAdapter.fetch_news`'s original 3C-i single try/except around the whole article loop was left unchanged; a malformed article invalidates that team's fetch for the cycle, handled identically to any other `ProviderError`. Explicitly flagged as a known asymmetry with Injury Worker's 3E-5 Decision 3 (per-row isolation) — changing NewsAPI's adapter contract wasn't authorized as part of this phase's scope.
  - **`app/persistence/daily_game_intelligence.py::write_news`:** scoped upsert touching only `game_id`/`news` (same `on_conflict=game_id`/`resolution=merge-duplicates` idiom `upsert_daily_game_intelligence` already uses) — proven by `test_write_touches_only_game_id_and_news_columns` and `test_rerun_upserts_rather_than_accumulating` (unlike Weather's append-only rows, reruns overwrite the same row, never accumulate).
  - **Two teams, one game, one combined+deduped `news` field:** a game's home/away teams' article lists are combined and de-duplicated by `url` before writing (proven by `test_two_teams_articles_combined_and_deduped_by_url`).

- **2026-08-18 — Phase 3E-8: Pregame Worker + Postgame Ingestion Worker implemented, tested, documented** (five architecture decisions approved by Mac before implementation, per his explicit "research first, do not begin implementation immediately" instruction).
  - **Pre-implementation research found three genuine architecture gaps, not assumptions:** (1) Pregame Worker's stated purpose ("catches last-minute inactive lists and line moves") was substantially already delivered by 3E-4/3E-5's own final-ramp cadence tiers — flagged as partially obsolete, not rebuilt from scratch. (2) `SportsDataIOTeamStatsAdapter`/`SportsDataIOPlayerStatsAdapter` already existed, built and fixture-tested in 3C-ii, never wired into a worker. (3) Game-final detection had no working mechanism at all: `_SCHEDULE_STATUS_MAP` only ever mapped `"Scheduled"` (the only value observed live, season hadn't started), and only Master Refresh's once-daily Schedule pass ever touched `games.status`. A fourth gap surfaced during research: `player_stats.player_id` is a hard `not null` FK, but `players` had never been populated by any code, and `players.external_provider_id` had the identical single-vendor defect `games`/`teams` both had before their own `_provider_ids` tables. All four presented to Mac as a formal execution plan before any code was written.
  - **Decision 1 (player identity, Option B) — solved, not deferred.** `player_provider_ids` (new table, mirrors `game_provider_ids`/`team_provider_ids` exactly) + `app/persistence/player_identity.py` (`resolve_player_ids`/`link_provider_player_id`/`ensure_player`). Population is `app/persistence/player_backfill.py`'s `PLAYER_BACKFILL` — exactly 4 real players, the entire universe of player identity evidence this project has ever captured (`rosters_normal.json`'s 2 rows: Xavier Worthy, Emmanuel Ogbah; `player_stats_week_bulk_normal.json`'s 2 rows: Josh Allen, Harold Landry III), each PlayerID/Name/Team/Position confirmed by direct fixture read. No live provider call made or authorized to expand coverage. Auto-creating a player opportunistically from live PlayerGameStatsByWeek data (not just the curated backfill) was considered and explicitly rejected — SportsDataIO Free Trial data is confirmed scrambled for several PlayerStats fields, and it would repeat the exact "expand identity without an explicit, reviewable backfill" pattern `team_backfill.py`'s own docstring already rejected once. Coverage gap reported (`unresolved_players`), never hidden.
  - **Decision 2 (game-final detection) — the smaller status-scan approach, not the full `GameFinished` event infrastructure.** No code anywhere in this codebase publishes a real Postgres LISTEN/NOTIFY event (confirmed before deciding). `app/workers/postgame_worker.py`'s `_detect_newly_final_games` re-polls the same `SportsDataIOScheduleAdapter`/`persist_schedule_entries` Master Refresh already uses (reused, not duplicated), scoped to a backward-looking window (`_RECONCILIATION_LOOKBACK_DAYS = 4`, DERIVED from Decision 5's own `+72h` checkpoint plus a one-day buffer, not a Blueprint number). `_SCHEDULE_STATUS_MAP` gains `"Final": "final"` — **explicitly ASSUMED / DEFERRED LIVE VERIFICATION, never presented as live-confirmed** — the 2026 season hadn't started as of the last live capture, so no completed-game status has ever actually been observed. The remaining SportsDataIO Free Trial call (11/12 used) was **not spent** to verify this: spending it now would very likely still only observe `"Scheduled"`, a calendar-timing problem independent of budget — the correct time is after a real game has finished, which costs no additional call. Detection and ingestion are two separate functions (`_detect_newly_final_games`/`_ingest_final_stats_for_game`) specifically so a future real event handler can call the ingestion half directly without redesigning the pipeline.
  - **Decision 3 (Pregame trigger) — T-minus-5-minutes, reusing the existing `Window.RAMP_5M` boundary**, not inventing a new timing concept (Volume 2 §8's Pregame row states no numeric value at all). `app/workers/pregame_worker.py` is a coordination worker only — it fetches nothing itself and owns no adapter. It forces one more, unconditional pass of `run_odds_worker`/`run_player_props_worker`/`run_injury_worker`/`run_weather_worker` for exactly the one game entering `Window.RAMP_5M`, via a new `target_game_ids: list[str] | None = None` parameter added to all four (backward-compatible — verified by the full pre-existing 50-test regression for those four workers passing unmodified with the parameter defaulted). News Worker is deliberately excluded — its own 3E-7 design means news relevance never expires at kickoff, so there's no T-minus-5-specific value to capture there. `triggered_game_ids` (caller-supplied, ephemeral, mirroring every other worker's `last_polled_at` convention) prevents re-triggering the same game within its 5-minute window.
  - **Decision 4 (targeted `daily_game_intelligence` refresh) — reused, not duplicated.** Master Refresh's own per-game assembly logic (`run_master_refresh`'s per-game loop) was extracted into `app/master_refresh/game_refresh.py::refresh_daily_game_intelligence_for_game` — behaviorally identical, proven by the full pre-existing Master Refresh test suite (11 tests) passing unmodified after the extraction. Pregame Worker calls it once for the triggering game immediately after its four-category refresh, rather than waiting for the next 6 AM run. A new `read_existing_players` (mirroring `read_existing_news`'s exact pattern) lets the extracted function read back `players` verbatim when `rosters` isn't supplied (Pregame's case — roster composition doesn't change in the final 5 minutes before kickoff).
  - **Decision 5 (bounded reconciliation) — a fixed, approved schedule, not a provider requirement.** `app/workers/reconciliation.py`: `initial`, `+10m`, `+30m`, `+2h`, `+24h`, `+72h`, in one place so the intervals can change centrally without rewriting the worker (Mac's explicit instruction). **Explicitly documented as an APPROVED PRODUCT/ARCHITECTURE DECISION, not SportsDataIO-confirmed** — PROGRESS.md's 2026-08-10 research cited a *range* from secondary-source vendor guidance ("10min-2h for the first 24h, tracked 48-72h"); this schedule is a related but distinct, concrete policy choice. After `+72h`, no further checks ever run (`is_reconciliation_complete`).
  - **Idempotent, correction-aware TeamStats/PlayerStats persistence — inspected Volume 3's intended meaning before implementing, per Mac's explicit instruction not to guess.** `team_stats`/`player_stats` have neither a uniqueness constraint nor an append-only DB trigger (confirmed by direct inspection; Volume 3 §6 already flags this as a standing, unclosed gap). Resolved without a schema change: `app/persistence/team_stats.py`/`player_stats.py` insert a new row only when incoming stats differ from the existing `created_at`-latest row for that (game, team)/(game, player) pair — reusing the same "latest row" read pattern `app/persistence/snapshots.py` already established for odds/injuries/weather, no new columns. This gives first-fetch-always-inserts, identical-reconciliation-check-inserts-nothing, and genuine-correction-inserts-a-new-row (original preserved) — all three properties Mac required. A DB-level append-only trigger (reusing the already-existing `block_snapshot_updates()` function) remains available future hardening, explicitly flagged for Mac's sign-off, not applied this phase — this was a real schema-adjacent decision surfaced and presented, not silently invented.
  - **Postgame Worker reuses 3C-ii's `SportsDataIOTeamStatsAdapter`/`SportsDataIOPlayerStatsAdapter` unchanged** — no second stats-fetch pipeline. `final_score` derived from `TeamGameStats`' own `Score`/`HomeOrAway` fields (confirmed present in the live-captured fixture) — no separate Scores/BoxScore endpoint added.

Updated in `app/adapters/providers/sportsdataio.py`'s module docstring and `_SCHEDULE_STATUS_MAP`'s own comment; `PROVENANCE.md` updated to match.
  3. **STOP — real architectural conflict found, not fixed.** `_SCHEDULE_STATUS_MAP` (`app/adapters/providers/sportsdataio.py`) maps only 2 of the 9 documented statuses (`Scheduled`, `Final`). Direct inspection of `SportsDataIOScheduleAdapter.fetch_schedule` (confirmed, not assumed): `_normalize_status` raises `ProviderDataError` for any unrecognized status value, and this raise is **not row-isolated** — it propagates out of the single loop building `entries` for the *entire* fetched Schedule response (a full-season fetch, ~304 games across every week, not just the requested slate). `ProviderDataError` is a subclass of `ProviderError`; both `run_master_refresh` (`except ProviderError` → `status="failed"`) and `run_postgame_worker`'s `_detect_newly_final_games` (`except (ProviderError, SchedulePersistenceError)` → `status="failed"`) catch it at the whole-run level, with no per-game fallback. **Two distinct problems, same root cause:**
     - **(a) Correctness bug, exactly the question asked:** `"F/OT"` (a completed overtime game) is never mapped to `"final"`. Even setting the crash aside, an overtime game would never be recognized as final by Postgame Worker's Schedule re-poll — its `games.status` would never transition, `finalized_at` would never be stamped, and postgame stats would never be ingested for that game. **Yes — current logic would fail to trigger postgame ingestion for an overtime game.**
     - **(b) Availability bug, broader than F/OT alone:** any of the other 7 unmapped values — most critically `"InProgress"`, which every single live game passes through during its own broadcast window — appearing anywhere in the full-season Schedule response aborts the *entire* fetch. On a real NFL Sunday, the moment any one game goes live, both Master Refresh and Postgame Worker would fail their entire run, not just skip that one game. This is more severe than the F/OT question alone and was found in the course of answering it.
     - **`games.status`'s own DB check constraint** (`supabase/migrations/20260807211306_sports_data_tables.sql`): `check (status in ('scheduled','live','final','postponed','canceled'))` — 5 values. `InProgress`→`live`, `Postponed`→`postponed`, `Canceled`→`canceled`, and `F/OT`→`final` all have existing, constraint-compatible internal equivalents. **`Suspended`, `Delayed`, and `Forfeit` have no existing internal equivalent** — a real decision needed (map to `live`/`postponed`/`final` respectively, as the closest existing semantics, or widen the constraint) before implementing anything.
     - **Smallest correct fix identified, NOT implemented, awaiting decision:** (i) expand `_SCHEDULE_STATUS_MAP` to cover all 9 values using the existing 5-value internal vocabulary wherever a direct equivalent exists (`InProgress`→`live`, `F/OT`→`final`, `Postponed`→`postponed`, `Canceled`→`canceled`); (ii) decide `Suspended`/`Delayed`/`Forfeit`'s internal mapping (recommend `Suspended`/`Delayed`→`live` since the game is not yet over, `Forfeit`→`final` since it's a terminal/completed state, no invented new value); (iii) separately, and more significantly, decide whether `fetch_schedule` should isolate a single malformed/unrecognized row (skip + report, mirroring Injury Worker's 3E-5 per-row isolation) rather than failing the entire batch — this is the deeper fix for problem (b) and changes Schedule's failure semantics for every consumer, a bigger design decision than the map expansion alone.
  4. **NFL rescheduling behavior CONFIRMED FROM PROVIDER DOCUMENTATION:** same-week reschedule keeps the same `GameID` (record updated in place); different-week reschedule marks the original `Postponed` and issues a new `GameID`/record. Cross-checked against `game_provider_ids`/`persist_schedule_entries`: **no new conflict** — same-week is already correctly handled by existing upsert-by-provider-id logic; different-week is also architecturally correct by construction (a new `GameID` naturally creates a new mapping/row), *provided* `Postponed` is a recognized status, which is the same shared fix as point 3, not a second independent issue.
  5. **Timezone CONFIRMED FROM PROVIDER DOCUMENTATION:** SportsDataIO NFL API times are Eastern (EST/EDT), DST handled by SportsDataIO. **No conflict, confirmed by direct inspection** — `fetch_schedule`/`_parse_timestamp_utc` exclusively read the provider's own `DateTimeUTC` field (already provider-converted to UTC); no code anywhere in this codebase reads the raw Eastern `DateTime` field. This confirmation explains why the existing UTC-normalization architecture is safe; nothing changed.
  6. **Free Trial scrambling reaffirmed** — matches this project's own independent cross-checks (documented since 3C-ii). No change to the existing discipline: trial captures are valid evidence for endpoint/schema shape, never for production-accurate statistical values.
  7. **NFL Timeframes endpoint + maintenance windows (first/third Wednesday monthly, 4 AM–10 AM Eastern) recorded** in `PROVENANCE.md` as CONFIRMED FROM PROVIDER DOCUMENTATION for future Railway-scheduling use. Not integrated — no scope expansion, per explicit instruction.
  - **Documentation updated:** `apps/sports-intel-layer/tests/fixtures/sportsdataio/PROVENANCE.md` (new sections for status vocabulary, rescheduling, timezone, Timeframes/maintenance windows); `app/adapters/providers/sportsdataio.py`'s module docstring and `_SCHEDULE_STATUS_MAP`'s own comment (documentation/comments only — the map itself is unchanged, per the STOP instruction). No CHANGELOG entry — no schema or shipped-behavior change this turn, comments/documentation only.

  - **Status mappings (all 9 documented values, using the existing 5-value internal vocabulary, no schema change):** `Scheduled`→`scheduled`, `InProgress`→`live`, `Final`→`final`, `F/OT`→`final`, `Postponed`→`postponed`, `Canceled`→`canceled`, `Delayed`→`scheduled`, `Suspended`→`live`, `Forfeit`→`final`. Mac's reasoning for the three previously-ambiguous values applied exactly as given: Delayed hasn't started and is still expected to play (closest existing state: scheduled); Suspended means play began and was halted (closest existing state: live); Forfeit is a completed result (final).
  - **Row isolation:** `SportsDataIOScheduleAdapter.fetch_schedule` now normalizes each Schedule row inside its own try/except — a failing row (unrecognized status/season_type/venue_type, or structurally malformed) is logged via `_logger.warning` (raw `GameKey`/`Status` included) and skipped; every other valid row in the same response still becomes a `ScheduleEntry`. HTTP failure, invalid top-level JSON, and non-array payloads still fail the whole call, unchanged — confirmed by dedicated tests. `app.master_refresh.run`'s own failure-isolation docstring corrected to match (Schedule row malformation moved from BLOCKING to NON-BLOCKING).

Core rule applied throughout: a malformed provider row must not destroy otherwise valid data from the same response or batch — same defensive philosophy as the Schedule fix, no vendor-shape assumptions changed.

  - **`fetch_team_stats`/`fetch_player_stats` (two-stage isolation):** because `rows` is the whole week's bulk payload shared across every game via `_WeeklyBulkCacheMixin`, a malformed row could previously abort the fetch for every game that week, not just the requested one — the most severe instance found. Fixed with a filtering stage + a model-building stage, each isolating independently.

Read-after-write within the same cycle: since this runs after every team's `persist_roster` attempt, a brand-new player's just-created mapping resolves the same cycle it was created, not one cycle later. A player with no confirmed mapping (never durably ingested, or this cycle's `persist_roster` failed before reaching them) gets `player_id: null` — the fresh roster/depth-chart data is still shown, never dropped, only the identity link is honest about not being confirmed. A failure of the batched lookup itself is non-blocking (new `MasterRefreshResult.player_id_resolution_failed` field, folds into `status="partial"`) — proven via a dedicated test where the lookup 500s and fresh roster data still reaches the DGI row with every `player_id` null.

  - **Gap #1 — cache hit-rate metric, CLOSED.** `app.adapters.cache.CacheMetrics` (hits/misses/sets) added, owned per-`CachingAdapter` instance — every worker already constructs one instance per category, so per-category independence is inherent, no second category-keyed store needed. Exact definitions: hit = `CacheBackend.get` returns non-`None`; miss = `None` for any reason (new key, expiry, or a fail-open read error); set = one write *attempt* (not a confirmed durable write — `RedisCacheBackend.set` already swallows write failures internally, unchanged). `hit_rate` returns `None` at zero samples, never a fabricated percentage. A separate `CacheBackend.errors` counter (only `RedisCacheBackend` ever increments it) tracks backend-level fail-open events distinctly from ordinary misses, exposed via `CachingAdapter.errors`. Purely additive — zero change to existing cache-aside semantics, confirmed by a dedicated test re-running the miss/hit/expiry sequence and checking actual returned values.

- **2026-08-20 — DEMO-3 (deterministic Demo Scenario Engine + Virtual Clock + Scenario Runner) BUILT — application code, tests, and full-regression proof only; live demo-Supabase proof not yet performed.** New package additions: `apps/sports-intel-layer/app/demo/scenario.py` (scenario schema), `app/demo/runner.py` (`ScenarioRunner`), `app/demo/reset.py` (operational-data reset + its own independent safety guard), `app/demo/scenarios/` (one bundled minimal scenario, `minimal_pregame_to_postgame.json`). No new worker, persistence, or Master Refresh module was created — the Scenario Runner drives the existing `run_master_refresh`/`run_odds_worker`/`run_player_props_worker`/`run_injury_worker`/`run_weather_worker`/`run_news_worker`/`run_pregame_worker`/`run_postgame_worker` entrypoints directly, through the real persistence layer, per the approved architecture.
  - **Architectural blocker discovered and resolved before Scenario Runner work began (STOP-and-report, Mac approved Option A):** every real worker entrypoint hardcoded its own real vendor adapter construction with no injection seam, making "pass a Demo*Adapter into the existing worker call" structurally impossible as originally scoped. Fix: 8 files (`app/workers/odds_worker.py`, `player_props_worker.py`, `injury_worker.py`, `weather_worker.py`, `news_worker.py`, `pregame_worker.py`, `postgame_worker.py`, `app/master_refresh/run.py`) each gained one or more optional, category-typed `*_adapter: XAdapter | None = None` parameters (`odds_adapter`, `player_props_adapter`, `injury_adapter`, `weather_adapter`, `schedule_adapter`, `roster_adapter`, `team_stats_adapter`, `player_stats_adapter`), used via `adapter = adapter or RealVendorAdapter(...)` — `None` (the default) preserves the exact prior construction and behavior for every existing caller, unchanged. Pregame/Postgame Worker needed no bigger redesign — pregame is pure passthrough to its 4 delegated workers' own new parameters; postgame's two private helpers (`_detect_newly_final_games`, `_ingest_final_stats_for_game`) each gained the matching optional parameter, threaded from the public `run_postgame_worker` signature. Documented as a general dependency-injection improvement, not Demo-specific business logic — no worker's real-provider behavior changed. Proof: 6 new tests in `tests/test_adapter_injection_seam.py`, using the same "point the unused real-vendor client at an unreachable host with zero respx route" technique already established, for both directions (no adapter injected → real endpoint IS contacted; adapter injected → real endpoint is NEVER contacted, identical result shape either way) plus a postgame/pregame passthrough proof. 3 pre-existing structural signature-assertion tests were updated (confirmed by reading each one's actual intent — "no unexpected vendor client," not "no new parameter at all") to include the new parameter names. **A genuine bug in DEMO-2's own already-"complete" `DemoOddsAdapter` was found and fixed in the same pass:** `fetch_odds([])` was returning empty instead of the full slate, breaking discovery-mode parity with `TheOddsApiOddsAdapter`'s own documented contract (empty list = full unfiltered slate, since the real bulk endpoint has no per-game filter) the moment it was actually exercised through the real Odds Worker for the first time — confined entirely to `app/demo/adapters.py`, fixed directly, one obsolete test replaced with one proving the corrected contract.
  - **Second genuine pre-existing production bug discovered and fixed, STOP-and-reported before touching it (Mac approved "fix it now"):** `app/persistence/daily_game_intelligence.py`'s `build_payload` called `_freshness_status(..., category="props")`, but `CATEGORY_TTL_SECONDS` (`app/adapters/cache.py`) only defines `"player_props"` — an unconditional `KeyError: 'props'` every time `daily_game_intelligence` assembly ran for a game with an actual player-prop snapshot on file. Never caught before because every pre-existing test in `tests/test_daily_game_intelligence_assembly.py` passed `props_row=None`; DEMO-3's scenario-runner integration test was the first thing in this codebase to ever drive a non-null props row all the way through Pregame Worker's targeted DGI refresh. Fix confined to that one file: the call site's `category="props"` → `category="player_props"`, and the co-located `_DEFAULT_VENDOR` lookup dict's key renamed to match (both are looked up by the same `category` argument, so they must agree). One new regression test (`test_props_row_present_assembles_without_raising`) proves the fix positively (status/source computed correctly, `CATEGORY_TTL_SECONDS["player_props"]` genuinely drives the freshness threshold — verified by mutating it and observing the status flip) and negatively (the old `category="props"` value still raises `KeyError`, confirming the fix isn't a coincidental pass). This is a general correctness fix, not Demo-specific — logged as its own dated note here since it's outside DEMO-3's own scope boundary, not folded into the DEMO-3 narrative.
  - **`app/demo/adapters.py` gained one more DEMO-3-driven addition, discovered the same way as the two bugs above (only once actually exercised end-to-end):** every `Demo*Adapter`'s `provider_name` is now an optional constructor parameter (default unchanged: `DEMO_PROVIDER_NAME = "demo"`, so all 33 DEMO-2 tests pass unmodified). Reason: several real, unmodified persistence modules (`game_identity.py`, `team_identity.py`, and three workers' own inline reverse-resolvers) key `game_provider_ids`/`team_provider_ids` identity-linkage rows by the literal real vendor name (`"sportsdataio"`, `"the_odds_api"`) taken from `AdapterResponse.source` — a Demo adapter that always reported `"demo"` would link identity rows under a name none of those real lookups ever query for, silently breaking cross-worker resolution (e.g. Player Props Worker could never find the game Odds Worker had just linked). `ScenarioRunner` passes the real vendor's own name per category (schedule/roster/injury/team-stats/player-stats → `"sportsdataio"`, odds/player-props → `"the_odds_api"`, weather → `"weatherapi"`, news → `"newsapi"`) — confined entirely to `app/demo/`, no real adapter or worker touched.
  - **`ScenarioRunner` (`app/demo/runner.py`):** owns `virtual_now` (moved only by explicit step data, never the wall clock) and in-memory control state (`status`, `step_index`, `outcomes`, `checkpoints`, `errors` — no new Supabase table, per Mac's stated in-memory preference). `load()`/`run_next_step()`/`run_to_completion()`. Dispatches the fixed `VALID_ACTIONS` vocabulary to the real worker entrypoints via the injection seam above; builds each step's `Demo*Adapter`(s) from that step's `provider_data` (empty/omitted categories default to an *empty* adapter, not DEMO-2's starter data — a step's data is meant to be authoritative for what it drives). The one required-but-unused real vendor client/key each worker signature still declares gets a placeholder (`https://demo-adapter-injected.invalid`, `"demo-unused-placeholder-key"`) — never a real credential, never a reachable host. **Discovered mid-build, fixed before it caused a real data bug (not just theoretical):** `app.adapters.cache.CachingAdapter`'s TTL expiry is real wall-clock time, oblivious to `virtual_now` — an early version sharing one `cache_backend` across a whole scenario run caused a later step's freshly-injected odds line move to be silently masked by an earlier step's still-real-time-fresh cached response. Fixed by giving every dispatched worker call its own brand-new `InMemoryCacheBackend` (documented in the class's own docstring); confirmed by a dedicated event-injection test.
  - **Scenario schema (`app/demo/scenario.py`):** plain dataclasses over JSON (no YAML, per Mac's instruction), `load_scenario(dict) -> Scenario`, eager validation (`ScenarioValidationError`) — unknown action, missing required field, malformed ISO datetime, empty `scenario_id`/`steps`, non-forward-moving step times, and a hard `phase_requirements` gate (only `"phase_3"` ever accepted — DEMO-3 cannot author a Phase 4/5 scenario even by mistake).
  - **Reset (`app/demo/reset.py`):** FK-safe delete order derived directly from the schema's own foreign keys (children before parents): `daily_game_intelligence, odds_snapshots, injury_reports, weather_snapshots, depth_chart_snapshots, team_stats, player_stats, roster_memberships, player_provider_ids, game_provider_ids, players, games` — `sports`/`leagues`/`seasons`/`teams`/`team_provider_ids` (the shared reference taxonomy) never appear in the list, never touched. **Independent second destructive-operation guard** (`assert_reset_is_safe`), separate code path from the existing startup-only `assert_demo_isolation`, checked fresh on every single reset call (not just once at process start) — hard-fails, zero DELETE issued, unless both `RAILWAY_ENVIRONMENT_NAME == "demo"` and `SUPABASE_URL` contain the approved demo project ref. Confirmed structurally (zero HTTP calls made when unsafe, via a zero-route respx context) and re-confirmed against dev-named / staging-named / production-named environment strings, and an empty/missing `SUPABASE_URL`.
  - **One minimal deterministic scenario built** (`minimal_pregame_to_postgame.json`, richer library is DEMO-5's job): a single synthetic KC @ BAL game built on the two real reference-taxonomy teams every environment already seeds (not fabricated teams — the FK-safe reset order treats `teams` as untouched reference data, so the scenario's `games`/`players` must resolve against teams that actually exist and survive reset unchanged; a fabricated "Demo Hawks" team would need its own reset-safe lifecycle DEMO-3 was never asked to build). 7 steps: Master Refresh (schedule + roster ingestion) → Odds Worker (opening moneyline) → Player Props Worker → Injury Worker (questionable) → News Worker → Pregame Worker (T-minus-5: line moves to -125, injury upgraded to out, targeted `daily_game_intelligence` refresh) → Postgame Worker (game goes final, KC 27–BAL 24, team/player stats ingested, `final_score` derived and persisted). Every team/player/game identifier chosen to match the real vendor identity conventions already seeded (`"KC"`/`"BAL"` for SportsDataIO-sourced categories, `"Kansas City Chiefs"`/`"Baltimore Ravens"` for The Odds API-sourced categories) so the real, unmodified identity-resolution code paths (never a demo-specific shortcut) do the linking.

Explicitly test-only, lives under `tests/`, never imported by `app/demo/*`, mirrors the existing `tests/adapters/fakes.py` precedent the user's own DEMO-2 spec pointed to. - **2026-08-20 — DEMO-5 (Phase 3 scenario library) BUILT — four new scenarios beyond DEMO-3's one minimal scenario, each proving a distinct real Phase 3 persistence/coordination behavior never previously exercised end-to-end. Approved 5-scenario library exactly as scoped (A = existing minimal scenario, unchanged; B-E new).** Fresh inspection preceded implementation per Mac's explicit "inspect first" instruction; no proposed scenario required changing production behavior to make it work — two genuine incompatibilities were found, both resolved at the scenario-JSON-authoring level, not by touching production code (see below).
  - **`pregame_intelligence_evolution.json` (Scenario B, 9 steps, Week 3 KC@BAL).** Odds (-120/100 → -145/125) and player props (1.5 → 2.5) each polled twice, proving append-only history (a new snapshot row, never an overwrite). Weather polled once (clear/68F), then 80 minutes of virtual time pass with zero further weather poll before a second `run_master_refresh` — proving `daily_game_intelligence`'s per-category freshness correctly flips from fresh to stale once the confirmed 15-minute weather TTL has elapsed.
  - **`team_news_injury_depth_chart.json` (Scenario C, 4 steps, Week 4 KC@BAL).** `injury_reports`' append-only escalation history (BAL QB `questionable` → `out`, the first report never overwritten). `roster_memberships`' genuine insert-on-team-*change* semantics proven against real persistence, not asserted from a docstring: a shared WR observed with BAL then KC gets two membership rows (a genuine team change); both QBs, observed with the same team twice, get exactly one row each. `depth_chart_snapshots` confirmed written unconditionally on every poll regardless of content change (2 Master Refresh calls × 2 teams = 4 rows).
  - **`provider_outage_resilience.json` (Scenario D, 2 steps, Week 5 KC@BAL).** The new `fail_categories: list[str]` mechanism (`ScenarioStep` field, `runner.py`'s `_should_fail` helper) isolates exactly the `injury` category at the Pregame Worker's single T-minus-5 coordinated refresh — a capability `inject_failure` alone couldn't express, since that worker builds four adapters (odds/player_props/injury/weather) from one step and `inject_failure=True` would fail all four. Proves odds/player_props/weather all still persist and `daily_game_intelligence` still refreshes for the three succeeding categories despite the simulated injury-provider outage.
  - **`postgame_stat_correction.json` (Scenario E, 3 steps, Week 6 KC@BAL).** The first scenario to exercise a second postgame reconciliation checkpoint beyond "initial" — the "+10m" checkpoint (the documented `("initial", 0), ("+10m", 10min), ...` schedule). KC's score/total_yards and the KC QB's passing_yards are corrected on the second poll while BAL's stats are re-submitted identical, proving `team_stats`/`player_stats`' insert-only-on-*change* persistence (a corrected team/player gets a new row; an unchanged one does not) and `games.final_score`'s recomputation/re-write when a correction changes the score.
  - **No dedicated postponed/canceled scenario built — per Mac's explicit agreement, not an oversight.** Demo Mode does not pretend there is special postponed/canceled handling the real Phase 3 backend doesn't have.
  - **Two genuine scenario-design incompatibilities found and resolved without touching production code, exactly per the "STOP and report, don't silently fix" discipline applied correctly here since neither was actually a production defect:**
    1. `run_master_refresh`'s own documented BLOCKING early-return (`if not slate_entries: return MasterRefreshResult(status="success", games_in_slate=0, ...)`, occurring before the per-game `daily_game_intelligence` reassembly loop) fires whenever that specific call's own schedule fetch is empty — correct, deliberate production behavior. Scenario B's and Scenario C's second `run_master_refresh` steps had each omitted re-supplying `"schedule"` in `provider_data` (wrongly assuming it wasn't needed since the game already existed), which silently skipped both DGI reassembly (B) and the roster-sync loop entirely (C, which sits after the same early-return). Root-caused via a standalone reproduction script dumping `fake.tables` state directly. Fixed by re-supplying the unchanged `"schedule"` entry in both steps' `provider_data` — a scenario-JSON fix, zero production code touched.
    2. `ScenarioRunner._build_weather_adapter` was missing the internal `game_id` → provider-`game_id` resolution hop `DemoWeatherAdapter`'s `WeatherConditions` model requires (`game_external_id` is a required field, the internal `games.id` UUID, unknown at scenario-authoring time) — a genuine Demo-mode-only gap, not a production defect (`weather_worker.py`'s own "no game_provider_ids hop" design is correct, deliberate production behavior). Fixed with a new async `ScenarioRunner._resolve_internal_game_id()` method querying `game_provider_ids`, zero production code touched.
  - **Real Phase 3 product/backend consideration surfaced during Scenario D's design, recorded here per explicit instruction rather than fixed inside Demo Mode:** postponed/canceled games receive no distinct handling in the Odds/Player Props/Injury/Weather Workers' candidate-game selection. `app.persistence.games.list_games_in_window` — the query all four of those workers call into to find due games — has no `status` filter at all (confirmed by direct code inspection of all four workers plus that persistence helper); a postponed/canceled game keeps getting polled by those four workers exactly like a normal scheduled one until it simply falls out of the kickoff-relative time window. Only two places in the whole codebase give `status` any special treatment: `find_previous_final_game` (`status='final'` filter, correctly excludes non-final games from rest-day computation) and the Postgame Worker's own `status == "final"` finalize gate. This is a genuine open question for later Phase 3 review (should those four workers stop polling a game once it's known postponed/canceled?) — not a bug introduced by Demo Mode, not solved here, not blocking Phase 4.

Neither model has a nullable field — a deliberate design decision, documented in the module docstring, carrying Milestone 4.1's null-not-neutral read-side discipline through to the output side: a `None` may flow into an agent's input, but an agent facing degraded/missing input must resolve that into a complete, valid judgment (typically low confidence + `evidence_classification="assumption"` + a `would_change_mind_if` naming the gap) rather than the contract accepting a partial/null shape as a substitute.

At these versions, both SDKs' `http_client` constructor parameter expects their own vendored `httpx2.AsyncClient` transport, not a plain `httpx.AsyncClient` -- making them respx-mockable (this codebase's established, universal adapter-testing convention) would require depending on that vendored transport type directly, for zero functional benefit at a milestone that makes zero live calls either way. **Decision: build `OpenAIModelAdapter`/`AnthropicModelAdapter` directly against each provider's public REST API via raw `httpx`** -- the identical convention every existing provider adapter in this codebase already uses (The Odds API, SportsDataIO, WeatherAPI, NewsAPI), fully respx-mockable, zero new SDK dependency.   - **A real schema gap discovered, flagged not silently worked around:** neither `model_routing_rules` nor `model_registry` (Volume 3 Section 8) has an explicit `provider` column -- `router.infer_provider` is an ASSUMED, name-prefix mapping (`"claude-*"` → anthropic, `"gpt-*"`/`"o1-*"`/`"o3-*"`/`"o4-*"`/`"chatgpt-*"` → openai), documented in the module docstring as a stopgap, not a permanent decision. **Recommend adding `model_registry.provider` as a schema follow-up before Milestone 4.4 wires real routing data against this** -- carried forward, not decided unilaterally.
  - **Retry/fallback matrix implemented exactly per requirement 9's table** (verbatim mapping, documented in full in `retry_policy.py`'s own module docstring): malformed output → one repair/retry against primary, then fallback; timeout → limited retry bounded by the elapsed-time budget, then fallback; 429 → `Retry-After` respected only within a configurable wait cap (default 10s) and only if it fits the remaining time budget, otherwise immediate fallback (avoids hammering); 5xx → limited retry with fixed backoff, then fallback; auth error → **no retry**, immediate fallback (a different provider may have valid credentials); other non-transient 4xx → **no retry**, immediate fallback. "Limited retry" is one shared, configurable `RetryFallbackPolicy.max_attempts_per_model` (default 2) applied uniformly, rather than three different unexplained magic numbers -- flagged as the simplest faithful reading of Mac's qualitative ("one," "limited") language, not a differing-per-type guess. `max_total_elapsed_seconds` (default 30s) is checked before every attempt, primary and fallback alike; exceeding it raises `ModelBudgetExceededError` immediately, skipping any remaining candidate. Exhausting every candidate without hitting the time budget raises `ModelAllAttemptsFailedError`, `__cause__` set to the last real underlying error. **"Repair" for malformed output is a same-request retry, not a re-prompt-with-the-validation-error-appended loop** -- flagged explicitly as a scope decision for a later milestone to refine, not silently assumed already built.
  - **Circuit breaker: seam only, not implemented, per requirement 10 and Mac's explicit instruction.** `CircuitBreaker` Protocol (`allow`/`record_outcome`) + `NoopCircuitBreaker` (always allows, tracks nothing) is the only implementation; `RetryEngine` depends on the Protocol, never the concrete class, so a future stateful breaker is a drop-in replacement requiring zero change to the retry engine's own call sites. Reasoned in the module docstring: Volume 2 Section 10's circuit-breaker requirement is about bounding a single runaway request (already covered by the elapsed-time/attempt-count budget), not necessarily a cross-request, provider-health-tracking breaker -- whether the stronger version is needed stays an open question for whichever milestone first runs real traffic.
  - **Usage/cost metadata contract (requirement 11):** `UsageMetadata` carries provider/model/input_tokens/output_tokens/total_tokens/latency_ms/attempt_count/used_fallback/estimated_cost_usd/correlation_id/task_type/agent_name. Every token/cost field defaults `None`, never `0` -- proven by dedicated tests on both the fake adapter and both real adapters (a provider response with no usage data produces `None` fields, never a fabricated number). `attempt_count`/`used_fallback` are populated only by `RetryEngine` (a bare single adapter call always reports `attempt_count=1, used_fallback=False`) -- the final returned usage reflects only the successful attempt's own tokens, never summed/fabricated from failed attempts (proven by a dedicated test: a 5xx failure then a success with `input_tokens=5, output_tokens=3` produces exactly those numbers, with `attempt_count=2`).
  - **Structured-output validation (requirement 6):** `parse_structured_output` (shared by fake and real adapters alike, zero duplicated logic) parses the raw text as JSON, then validates against whatever `ModelRequest.response_model` the caller supplied -- not-JSON-at-all, a missing required field, an invalid enum value, an out-of-bounds number, or an unrecognized extra field (`AgentOutput`/`MetaAgentOutput`'s own `extra="forbid"`) all raise `ModelMalformedOutputError` explicitly, never a guessed/filled-in default. Proven end-to-end (`tests/models/test_agent_contract_integration.py`): a real `AgentOutput` validates correctly through `FakeModelAdapter` + `RetryEngine`; a malformed first attempt is correctly repaired by one retry; a genuinely incomplete output exhausts retries and raises cleanly with no fallback configured.

Read literally, this is exactly the condition Mac's instruction said to stop on. **However: Milestone 4.4 as scoped and as actually built never writes to `recommendation_agent_outputs` or `consensus_snapshots` at all** — the four agents produce in-memory `AgentOutput` objects via the fan-out executor only; no persistence-write path for agent outputs exists yet in this codebase. The FK constraint is real but is not exercised by any code this milestone adds, so no schema conflict was actually triggered by this build, and no schema change was made. **For whenever a future milestone (projected: 4.5+) does wire real persistence of agent outputs, the recommended resolution is Option C from Mac's own list**: reinterpret `recommendations` as a neutral recommendation-cycle/run container that Phase 4 creates early (populating `game_id` and the versioning columns — Volume 3's own text already states "`prompt_version` and `agent_version` are populated by the Orchestrator at the moment of creation," which only makes sense if Phase 4 creates the row) and that Phase 5 completes progressively via `UPDATE` (populating `recommendation_type`, `bet_details`, `status`, and the other Phase-5-owned fields, all of which are confirmed nullable — no trigger blocks a progressive update). This interpretation is directly supported by the schema as it exists today and requires no migration. **This is a recommendation, not a decision made on Mac's behalf — it needs his explicit confirmation before any future milestone actually persists agent outputs against it**, exactly as flagged in the completion report delivered alongside this entry.

    - **`TravelFatigueAgent`** — evidence: the four deterministic travel fields (below) plus `rest`, for reasoning about combined travel-and-rest fatigue significance.

  - **Async fan-out executor — Decision 8.** `app/orchestration/fanout.py` (new): `run_agent()` wraps the full route→adapter-lookup→`RetryEngine.execute()` flow in a deliberate broad `except Exception` so it **structurally can never raise** — every failure (routing error, missing adapter registration, exhausted retries) becomes a returned `AgentRunResult(status="failed", error=...)`, never a fabricated zero-confidence output. `run_fan_out()` runs all agents concurrently via `asyncio.gather` and computes **FULL** (every agent succeeded), **PARTIAL** (at least one succeeded, at least one failed), or **FAILED** (zero succeeded) — structural conditions, not a chosen quorum threshold; an actual minimum-viable-participation number is explicitly carried forward to Milestone 4.7 (Consensus Engine), not invented here, per Mac's instruction. 7 new tests (`tests/orchestration/test_fanout.py`): single-agent success, single-agent total-failure-never-raises, missing-adapter-registration-reported-as-failed, FULL/PARTIAL/FAILED for 2-agent fan-outs (the PARTIAL test uses one shared adapter whose behavior branches per-agent, proving isolation is per-agent not per-adapter, and asserts the failed agent's `output` stays `None`), and a real-concurrency proof (4 agents each with a genuine `asyncio.sleep(0.05)` delay complete in well under the ~0.20s a sequential run would take).

That does NOT mean P(bet wins) = 0.80"; "Do NOT semantically reuse AgentOutput.confidence as p_model"). Ten decisions (A-G plus routing/persistence corrections) resolved the revised design; Decision G (target market/candidate identity) required a dedicated persistence-schema inspection before implementation, per Mac's explicit STOP-and-report instruction — see below.
  - **Decision A (sequential context) — approved and built exactly as proposed:** a new `SequentialDecisionContext`/`SequentialDecisionAgent` pair, structurally distinct from `AgentContext`/`ContextDataAgent` — these four agents consume the committee's own outputs and deterministic downstream math, never per-game raw facts.
  - **Decision B (Probability Modeling) — Mac's correction, not my original proposal.** `app/agents/probability_output.py`'s new `ProbabilityModelOutput` (Pydantic, `extra="forbid"`): `agent_name`, `candidate_key`, `selection`, `modeled_probability` (0.0-1.0), `confidence_in_probability` (0.0-1.0, a genuinely different number), `reasoning`, `supporting_evidence`, `would_change_mind_if`. `AgentOutput.confidence` is completely untouched everywhere else. Volume 4 Section 4.1's consensus formula is explicitly NOT pulled forward into this milestone — it remains Milestone 4.7's job alone.
  - **Decision C (EV) — approved with terminology correction applied.** `app/features/probability.py` (American↔decimal, American↔implied-probability) + `app/features/expected_value.py` (`compute_ev`): `ev_per_dollar = p_model × decimal_odds - 1` against the actual offered price; `raw_probability_edge` explicitly labeled vig-inclusive, never presented as a de-vigged fair-market edge (de-vigging deliberately deferred, not built). `american_odds == 0` raises `InvalidOddsError`, never silently coerced; missing odds degrades every field to `None`.
  - **Decision D (Kelly/risk tolerance) — approved with product-policy correction.** `app/features/kelly.py`: standard closed-form `full_kelly = (b*p-q)/b` (asserted by test to equal `ev_per_dollar/b`, an algebraic identity), `quarter_kelly = max(full_kelly, 0) × 0.25`. `RISK_TOLERANCE_MULTIPLIERS` explicitly documented as initial product-policy configuration, not math: `conservative=0.50` (1/8 Kelly), `moderate=0.75` (3/16 Kelly), `aggressive=1.00` (full quarter-Kelly) — no profile exceeds quarter-Kelly in V1. `preferred_unit_size` remains deliberately unused — no exact role for it has been decided, and none was invented here.
  - **Decision E (Risk Manager) — approved, DEGRADED, correctly named.** `app/features/risk.py`: `bernoulli_outcome_variance = p*(1-p)`, a real computable statistical fact — explicitly NOT presented as Volume 4 Section 2.5's named "historical variance by bet type," which is confirmed (direct schema grep, repeated from the pre-4.6 inspection) to not exist anywhere in this schema. `historical_bet_type_variance` is always `None`, reported honestly, never fabricated.
  - **Decision F (Bankroll Coach) — approved, synthetic-profile tested.** New `app/persistence/user_profiles.py` (`read_user_profile`, read-only, matches this package's other readers' exact convention) confirms live in dev: **every real `user_profiles` row has `optional_bankroll IS NULL`**, including both Phase-1 fixture rows — there is no row in this database with a real bankroll number. `app/features/kelly.compute_stake` treats `bankroll=None`/`bankroll<=0` as `stake=None` as a first-class path, proven by test alongside a synthetic-complete-profile → valid-stake test. Real Kelly *fractions* (not the dollar stake) still compute and are shown even without a bankroll.
  - **Decision G (target market/candidate identity) — the most consequential finding, required a dedicated persistence-schema STOP before any candidate code was written.** Nothing in Volume 4 or Volume 2 Section 4.4 specifies which market a committee run evaluates a probability *for* — `AgentOutput.directional_lean`'s enum can only speak to one side at a time. **Persistence-schema inspection result (performed live against dev before implementation, per Mac's explicit instruction):** `recommendation_agent_outputs` has no unique constraint blocking multiple rows per `(recommendation_id, agent_id)` (technically permissive), but also **no column identifying which candidate a row belongs to** — that identity would have had to live entirely inside unindexed `raw_output` JSON, with no query support, no index, and no guard against ambiguous duplicates. Two clean options were presented (embed-in-JSON-only vs. a small additive `candidate_key` column); a third (one `recommendations` row per candidate) was explicitly rejected as fighting Phase 5's own job of comparing many candidates down to few final recommendations. **Mac approved Option 2.** `supabase/migrations/20260822140000_recommendation_agent_outputs_candidate_key.sql`: nullable `candidate_key text` + a partial index `(recommendation_id, candidate_key) where candidate_key is not null` — no uniqueness constraint (multiple evaluations of the same candidate over time are legitimate history, not an error; retry/versioning semantics for this identity are not yet designed strongly enough to justify one). Applied live to dev, verified, mirrored in the repo migration history. Volume 3 → v4.14 (MINOR), `CHANGELOG.md` v4.14 (Volume 3) entry recorded.

Proves the required distinction: a deferred agent (never built, e.g. Referee Tendencies) never appears in `attempted_agents`/`failed_agents`, distinct from a built agent that ran and genuinely failed this cycle.

If Probability Modeling succeeds, deterministic EV/variance/Kelly math is computed in application code **between** agent calls (mirroring `build_agent_context`'s `TravelFeatures` precedent) and persists regardless of whether that step's own LLM narration call succeeds — proven by a test where the Expected Value agent's LLM call fails but Risk Manager/Bankroll Coach still receive real, correctly-computed EV numbers in their evidence.

No uniqueness constraint, same partial-index pattern as Decision G. Volume 3 → v4.15 (MINOR), `CHANGELOG.md` entry recorded. Applied live to dev, verified.
  - **Decision I (directional agreement) — approved: candidate-anchored consensus, an intentional Blueprint evolution.** Volume 4 §4.1's original "majority lean" wording predates any candidate concept and, taken literally, would let off-axis agents (e.g. a totals-relevant lean) outvote on-axis ones for a moneyline candidate. `app/features/consensus.py`'s `resolve_candidate_direction()` maps a moneyline/spread candidate's `selection` (a team **name**) against the game's authoritative `home_team`/`away_team` via exact string match only (never array position or text ordering, per Mac's explicit instruction) — raising `CandidateDirectionError` for a genuinely malformed non-matching selection, never silently treating it as "no vote." Totals map `"Over"`/`"Under"` directly. **Player-prop candidates return `None` (no directional mapping exists) — explicitly excluded, not forced through with an invented mapping**, per Mac's explicit instruction; every agent becomes a non-voter for a prop candidate, so `aggregate_confidence` is `None` (undefined, not fabricated) for one.
  - **The exact three-state deterministic formula, documented per Mac's own "if ambiguous, stop and document it" instruction:** `lean_factor()` returns `1.0` (matches), `0.3` (exact opposite on the same axis), or `None` ("no directional vote" — covering both `directional_lean="none"` and, by the same reasoning Mac stated for "none," an off-axis lean that simply isn't expressible on this candidate's own question). Non-voting agents are excluded from both the numerator and denominator of `aggregate_confidence` and from `agreement_variance`'s sample — never coerced into support or opposition — while remaining fully visible in the persisted `participation_metadata`.
  - **A second, real gap found and resolved the same way (no-result-no-row), not silently worked around:** `consensus_snapshots.aggregate_confidence` is `NOT NULL` (the original Phase-1 column) — `compute_consensus` can legitimately return `None` (zero voting agents, zero successful fan-out participants, or a prop candidate). Resolution: no `consensus_snapshots` row is written at all when this happens — the same "failed agent, no row" pattern already established at Milestones 4.4/4.5, applied here for the first time to a table-level `NOT NULL` constraint. Documented explicitly in `app/persistence/consensus_snapshots.py`'s own module docstring, not silently coded around.
  - **A third, real gap found while implementing Decision I's threshold check, flagged rather than silently resolved:** with only two possible `lean_factor` values (`1.0`, `0.3`), the maximum possible population variance of any real distribution is `p(1-p)(1.0-0.3)^2`, maximized at a 50/50 split: `0.25 × 0.49 = 0.1225` — meaning Volume 4 §4.3's own `agreement_variance > 0.25` Elite second-pass threshold can **never fire from any real computed input**, under Volume 4's own specified `0.3` fractional disagreement penalty, independent of and predating this milestone's candidate-anchoring redesign (the original game-level majority-vote formula has the identical two-value ceiling). Verified numerically (`p*(1-p)*(1.0-0.3)**2` swept over `p` in `[0,1]`, confirmed max `0.1225`). **Not silently resolved** — `should_trigger_elite_second_pass()`'s trigger LOGIC is implemented and tested correctly in isolation (directly-supplied variance values, since no real computed input can reach the threshold today); Volume 4 §4.1/§4.3 (→ v5.1, MINOR) now document this as an open question for Mac (whether the fractional penalty, the `0.25` threshold, or the variance formula itself should change) rather than picking one silently. `CHANGELOG.md` v5.1 (Volume 4) entry recorded.
  - **Decision J (weight source) — approved, implemented exactly as specified.** New `app/persistence/consensus_snapshots.read_game_level_agent_outputs()` reads back the already-persisted, `candidate_key IS NULL` game-level fan-out rows (embedding `agents(name, category)` via PostgREST's FK-based embedding for the Meta Agent's category-clustering evidence, no second round-trip) — sourcing `weight_applied` (frozen at fan-out time) and `agent_confidence`, never a fresh `agents.current_weight` join. A dedicated test proves zero `GET /rest/v1/agents` calls occur anywhere in the consensus run. The `evidence_classification="assumption"` → 0.5× effective-weight discount is applied without ever mutating the stored `weight_applied` value (also tested directly).

Routes via the `consensus_reconciliation` task_type, already seeded in dev config since Milestone 4.1.
  - **`final_aggregate_confidence` computed exactly per Mac's formula:** `apply_confidence_adjustment(current, adjustment) = max(0.0, current + adjustment)`, applied first for Meta's adjustment, then (only if Elite second-pass triggers) again for Elite's — guaranteeing `final_aggregate_confidence` can never exceed `aggregate_confidence` at any point (both adjustments are `<=0`, both clamps only lower). A failed Meta or Elite LLM call (e.g. a rejected positive-adjustment attempt) degrades to a `0.0` adjustment for that step rather than blocking consensus persistence — Meta/Elite are QC layers on top of already-valid deterministic math, not a prerequisite for it (unlike Milestone 4.6's Probability Modeling).

The Recommendation Worker derives `correlation_id = f"{master_refresh_run_id}:{game_id}"` — stable, deterministic, no wall-clock component.
  - **V1 candidate generation (`app/features/candidate_generation.py`, ai-orchestrator, new) — the critical policy gap Mac's inspection questions flagged before any candidate code was written.** Six V1 candidates at most per game (home/away moneyline, home/away spread, over/under total), generated from the LATEST fresh snapshot of ONE reference sportsbook selected by walking a configured ordered preference list (`REFERENCE_SPORTSBOOK_PREFERENCE`, `app/config.py`) — no book-shopping across markets within a game, an individual missing/stale market degrades gracefully (skipped, honest reason recorded) without blocking the other markets from the same book, and the whole game is skipped only when literally no configured book has any fresh data. Freshness reuses sports-intel-layer's own `classify_window` polling-cadence tiers (duplicated per this codebase's cross-service convention) as a read-side staleness ceiling, plus an explicit `ORCHESTRATION_GRACE_SECONDS=300` buffer for the Worker's own post-refresh latency. Player props explicitly excluded — the Player Prop Agent doesn't exist, prop consensus semantics are unresolved. No pre-selection of "the winner" — both sides of every market are independent candidates with their own real prices, never a single "favorite" candidate. 17 tests.
  - **Decision 2 (shared-vs-personalized execution split, Volume 4 v5.3) — the largest architectural change this milestone, closing a real inefficiency the pre-4.9 shape would have hit immediately once generation went proactive/multi-user.** Pre-4.9, one sequential chain bundled Probability Modeling→EV→Risk→Bankroll Coach behind a single `user_id`, and consensus's Elite trigger check was similarly keyed off one `user_id` — meaning a second user viewing an already-evaluated candidate would re-run real Probability/EV/Risk/Elite model calls for identical football evidence. Split: `app/orchestration/sequential.py`'s `run_shared_candidate_chain` (Probability→EV→Risk, no user concept, runs once per candidate) + `run_bankroll_coach_step` (per-user, reuses the shared chain's own returned context); `app/orchestration/cycle.py`'s `run_candidate_evaluation` (shared) + new `run_bankroll_coach_evaluation` (per-user). `app/orchestration/consensus.py` split three ways: `run_shared_consensus` (consensus + Meta Agent, no user/tier concept, never persists), `run_elite_reconciliation` (takes a caller-resolved `tier`, callable at most once per candidate — the caller, not this module, is responsible for invoking it once regardless of how many Elite users are viewing the candidate), `finalize_consensus` (the single persistence call, made once after both steps complete). **Deliberately did NOT add a uniqueness constraint to `consensus_snapshots` to enable an upsert** — would have silently reversed a decision already documented in that table's own migration ("no uniqueness constraint… retry/versioning semantics aren't designed yet either"); the "compute once, reuse" guarantee is instead enforced by orchestration-level sequencing discipline alone, zero schema change. Caught and fixed a real bug during this work: `run_game_recommendation` was initially passing `AgentRunResult` wrapper objects into `upstream_outputs` instead of their `.output` — caught by the new integration tests, not by inspection.

"Active subscribers" resolved once per game via new `app.persistence.subscriptions.read_active_subscribers` (`subscriptions.status='active'`) — **Mac's approved answer (2026-08-24) to a genuine gap surfaced mid-implementation:** no user-enumeration capability existed anywhere in this codebase before this milestone, and Volume 4 §3.1's "the worker iterates active users" never specified the concrete mechanism; flagged and asked rather than guessed. Per-candidate and per-subscriber failure isolation implemented via dedicated try/except boundaries in `_evaluate_one_candidate`/the Bankroll Coach loop — proven by dedicated tests (one candidate's simulated failure doesn't block the others; one subscriber's simulated Bankroll Coach failure doesn't block the next). Real adapter registry (`AnthropicModelAdapter`/`OpenAIModelAdapter`) constructed lazily per-request from `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY`, never at import time — a missing key means an absent provider, isolated per-agent exactly like every other missing-config case in this codebase, never a startup crash. 12 new tests (`test_recommendation_worker.py`) + 3 endpoint tests.

Fallback-provider retries would add up to another full pass per call category on top of the above.

Both surfaced explicitly rather than silently worked around. Mac's resolution (Decisions P-AN, across four separate approval rounds — architecture direction, Revised Milestone Map, Strategy Architecture Decisions, and a Final Pre-Migration DDL Inspection with 9 proven invariants): preserve Phase 4 exactly, add a new product layer above it with first-class leg provenance, never opaque JSON; fix the step-order contradiction; qualification requires both `final_aggregate_confidence >= 0.55` AND `ev_per_dollar > 0`; ranking/tie-break/same-market-conflict-resolution all use one hierarchy (`ev_per_dollar` DESC, `final_aggregate_confidence` DESC, `candidate_key` ASC, the last purely for determinism); `no_bet` is per-game, `bankroll_preservation` is slate-wide, `single`-vs-`multiple_singles` is decided by total leg count; parlays stay schema-supported but inactive (confirmed via a full-codebase grep — zero joint-probability/combined-EV/correlation/sportsbook-parlay-pricing logic exists anywhere); `user_recommendation_selections` must be append-only with a genuinely atomic materiality gate, not update-in-place; `recommendation_legs` must represent only actually-selected wagers, never a fake `candidate_key=NULL` placeholder for `no_bet` explainability (an explicit correction to an earlier draft of this schema); `display_id` generation must be proven race-free via a real atomic Postgres mechanism, not an application-level read-then-write.
  - **Milestone 5.1 implementation, following the DDL inspection finding no new conflict:** migrations `20260825120000_recommendation_products_schema.sql` and `20260825130000_display_id_counter_function.sql` (plus a follow-up pinning `search_path` on the 4 new trigger functions) applied live to dev (`nhwjtsdebgiwskshzqiq`) — 5 new tables (`recommendation_products`, `recommendation_legs`, `user_recommendation_selections`, `display_id_counters`, `conversations`), Volume 3 → v4.18. A rollback-wrapped live SQL proof against dev confirmed every mechanism fires correctly under real inserts: both append-only triggers block their forbidden updates while `recommendation_products`' one legitimate withdrawal mutation succeeds; `idx_recommendation_legs_one_per_market` rejects an opposing-side moneyline leg; the materiality gate wrote exactly 2 rows for 3 attempted `user_recommendation_selections` inserts (suppressing only the identical repeat, keeping the genuine risk-tolerance change); the atomic `display_id` counter produced two distinct, non-colliding values.
  - **Code:** `apps/ai-orchestrator/app/features/strategy.py` (new, pure — qualification, ranking/tie-break, same-market conflict resolution, the full no_bet/single/multiple_singles/bankroll_preservation decision tree; 17 hand-computed tests), `app/persistence/recommendation_products.py` (new — display_id generation via the atomic RPC, product/leg persistence for every outcome shape; 7 tests), `app/orchestration/strategy_finalize.py` (new — ties the pure decision to persistence for one slate), a new `POST /v1/internal/recommendation-worker/finalize-strategy` endpoint (ai-orchestrator, 4 tests). `app/persistence/consensus_snapshots.py`'s `persist_consensus_snapshot` and `app/orchestration/consensus.py`'s `finalize_consensus` now return the persisted `consensus_snapshot_id` (additive — no behavior change, only what's returned) so a leg can anchor to the exact frozen evaluation it came from, never an ambiguous `(recommendation_id, candidate_key)` pair (which isn't unique — an Elite second-pass writes a new snapshot row rather than updating). `app/orchestration/recommendation_worker.py`'s per-candidate loop now also builds a `strategy_input` from the SAME in-memory `candidate`/EV/consensus objects that cycle already computed — never re-read back from persistence, so Strategy sees exactly what was evaluated, never a later, possibly-moved price. `apps/workers/app/ai_orchestrator_client.py`/`recommendation_worker.py` relay each dispatched game's `strategy_input` list, unmodified, into one `finalize-strategy` call per cycle after every game's dispatch completes (a game that failed to dispatch is omitted, never represented as `no_bet` — it was never evaluated at all); the finalize-strategy call's own failure is isolated into a `strategy_error` field, never allowed to mark an already-successful game dispatch as failed.
  - **Why a slate-level relay through `apps/workers`, not a bigger change:** `multiple_singles`/`bankroll_preservation` need to see every game in a slate at once, which the existing per-game `run_game_recommendation` structurally can't provide alone. `apps/workers` already owns exactly this cross-game relay role (deciding which games are eligible, dispatching one call per game, isolating each one's failure) — extending it to also relay results into one slate-level Strategy call after every game's dispatch completes is the same relay role, not new business logic; Strategy's own decision logic still lives entirely in `ai-orchestrator`.

Both real adapters send `request.model` literally to the provider API. Invisible until Milestone 5.3's own new fallback test exercised this path for the first time — zero live impact today since every model call in this codebase still uses `FakeModelAdapter`. `used_fallback` is still correctly recorded (comes from the retry engine's own bookkeeping); `model_name` in that scenario is provably wrong, captured exactly as-is in `tests/orchestration/test_fanout.py::test_run_agent_used_fallback_flag_is_correct_even_though_model_name_is_not` rather than silently asserting a corrected value the codebase doesn't produce. This does not verify every individual reconciliation checkpoint actually ran successfully, only that enough wall-clock time has passed for all of them to have had their chance — documented in Volume 3 §5D, Volume 4 §9.6, and the roadmap's own Milestone 5.4 correction note.

  - **A real sign-convention bug found and fixed during self-testing, not from user feedback:** the first implementation applied a naive symmetric rule (`correct`→exact leg P&L, `underperforming`→negated leg P&L) regardless of WIN/LOSS. On a LOSS leg this inverted the reward direction — rewarding agreement with the losing pick, penalizing agreement with the (unpriced) winning side — because the graded leg's own frozen price is only exactly known for the side it represents. Rewrote `classify_and_price_observation` to the correct truth table (exact leg P&L only for `correct`+`WIN` and `underperforming`+`LOSS`, a flat ±1-unit proxy for the other two combinations, since no second market price exists in this system), rewrote the corresponding tests, all 26 feature tests then passed.

Nothing persisted (`ROLLBACK`).
